**46** Do not let your heart faint, and do not be fearful at the report that is heard in the land, when a report comes in one year and afterward a report in another year, and violence is in the land, and ruler is against ruler.

**47** Therefore, see, the days are coming when I will punish the images of Babylon. Her whole land will be put to shame, and all her slain will fall in the midst of her.

**48** Then the heavens and the earth, and all that is in them, will sing for joy over Babylon, for the destroyers will come against them out of the north," declares the LORD.

**49** "Babylon must fall for the slain of Israel, as for Babylon have fallen the slain of all the earth.

**50** You who have escaped from the sword, go, do not stand still. Remember the LORD from far away, and let Jerusalem come into your mind:

**51** 'We are ashamed, for we have heard reproach. Dishonour has covered our face, for foreigners have come into the holy \[H-QADOSH\] places of the LORD's house.'

**52** Therefore, see, the days are coming," declares the LORD, "when I will punish her images, and through all her land the wounded will groan.

**53** Though Babylon should mount up to heaven, and though she should fortify her strong height, yet from me destroyers would come to her," declares the LORD.

**54** A voice. A cry from Babylon. The noise of great destruction from the land of the Chaldeans.

**55** For the LORD is laying Babylon waste and stilling her loud voice. Their waves roar like many waters. The noise of their voice is raised,

**56** for a destroyer has come on her, on Babylon. Her warriors are taken. Their bows are broken in pieces, for the LORD is a God of recompense. He will surely repay.

**57** I will make drunk her officials and her wise, her governors, her commanders and her warriors. They will sleep a perpetual sleep and not wake," declares the King, whose name is the LORD of hosts.

**58** Thus says the LORD of hosts: "The broad wall of Babylon will be levelled to the ground, and her high gates will be burned with fire. The peoples labour for nothing, and the nations weary themselves only for fire."

**59** The word that Jeremiah the prophet commanded Seraiah the son of Neriah, son of Mahseiah, when he went with Zedekiah king of Judah to Babylon, in the fourth year of his reign. Seraiah was the quartermaster.

**60** Jeremiah wrote in a book all the disaster that would come on Babylon, all these words that are written concerning Babylon.

**61** And Jeremiah said to Seraiah: "When you come to Babylon, see that you read all these words aloud,

**62** and say, 'LORD, you have said concerning this place that you will cut it off, so that nothing will live in it, neither human nor animal, and it will be desolate for ever.'

**63** When you finish reading this book, tie a stone to it and cast it into the midst of the Euphrates,

**64** and say, 'Thus Babylon will sink, to rise no more, because of the disaster that I am bringing on her, and they will weary themselves.'" Thus far are the words of Jeremiah.

*Review note: Jeremiah 46 to 51 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the oracles against the nations in the Hebrew order: Egypt (46:1-28), the Philistines (47:1-7), Moab (48:1-47), Ammon (49:1-6), Edom (49:7-22), Damascus (49:23-27), Kedar and Hazor (49:28-33), Elam (49:34-39) and Babylon (50:1 to 51:64), with the word that was sunk in the Euphrates (51:59-64). The oracles are poetry, apart from the headings and 51:59-64. In the Greek the oracles are in a different order and stand after 25:13, and the footnotes record the matter. The oracles are dated in places: Egypt to the fourth year of Jehoiakim (605 before Christ, 46:2), Elam to the beginning of the reign of Zedekiah (49:34) and the sinking of the scroll to the fourth year of Zedekiah (593 before Christ, 51:59). In 46:26, 49:37 and 51:6 and 51:45 "life" and "their life" are tagged H-NEPHESH, and in 50:19 "his desire" is tagged H-NEPHESH. In 48:47, 49:6 and 49:39 "I will restore the fortunes" is tagged H-SHUV, with the footnote on *shuv shevut* (30:3). In 49:36, 51:1 and 51:11 and 51:16-17 *ruach* is tagged H-RUACH, with "winds", "spirit", "wind" and "breath" as the sense requires. In 50:5 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT, in 50:7 and 51:10 "righteousness" and "vindication" are tagged H-TSEDAQAH, in 50:29 and 51:5 and 51:51 "Holy One of Israel" and "holy places" are tagged H-QADOSH, and in 50:34 "Redeemer" is tagged H-GOEL. In 46:27-28 the verses are as in 30:10-11, and the wording is matched. In 50:41-43 the verses are as in 6:22-24, and the wording is matched. In 50:44-46 the verses are as in 49:19-21, and the wording is matched. In 51:15-19 the verses are as in 10:12-16, and the wording is matched. In 49:18 and 50:40 the verses on Sodom and Gomorrah are as the Hebrew has them in each place, and the footnote records the difference. In 49:7-22 the oracle against Edom is close to Obadiah 1-9, and the wording is open to be matched when Obadiah is drafted. In 48:1-47 the oracle against Moab uses lines of Isaiah 15 and 16 and of Numbers 21:28-29, and the wording is open to be matched with Isaiah 15 and 16. In 49:1 and 49:3 "Milcom" is read for the Hebrew vowel pointing *malkam* ("their king"), which has the same letters, and the footnote records the matter. In 50:38 "a drought" (choreb) is read for the Hebrew vowel pointing cherev ("a sword"), which has the same letters, and the footnote records the matter. In 51:1 "Leb-qamai" and in 51:41 and 25:26 "Sheshach" are given as the Hebrew has them, and the footnotes record that they are coded names for the Chaldeans and for Babylon. The oracles against the nations, with the words on Babylon as a drunken cup and on Bel, are rendered as written. *At 46:15 the WABV text gives "Why has Apis fled? Why did your bull not stand?", following the Greek, where the Hebrew text has "Why has your strong one been swept away? He did not stand." (*maddua nisechaph abbirekha lo amad*), and it departs from the Hebrew. It is the one departure in chapters 46 to 51, and it is flagged for the scholar.* The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 46:5, 46:15-17, 46:20-22, 47:3-5, 48:2, 48:6, 48:11, 48:26, 48:30, 48:36-39, 48:45, 49:1, 49:8, 49:19-21, 49:25, 49:30, 49:32, 50:6, 50:9, 50:21, 50:24, 50:38, 51:1-3, 51:11, 51:13, 51:30-32 and 51:55-58. In 51:2 "winnowers" is read for the Hebrew vowel pointing zarim ("strangers"), with the same letters, and the footnote records the matter. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Ten: The Fall of Jerusalem Retold (Chapter 52)

### Chapter 52

**1** Zedekiah was twenty-one years old when he began to reign, and he reigned eleven years in Jerusalem. His mother's name was Hamutal the daughter of Jeremiah of Libnah.

**2** And he did what was evil in the sight of the LORD, according to all that Jehoiakim had done.

**3** For because of the anger of the LORD things came to the point in Jerusalem and Judah that he cast them out from his presence. And Zedekiah rebelled against the king of Babylon.

**4** And in the ninth year of his reign, in the tenth month, on the tenth day of the month, Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon and all his army came against Jerusalem and laid siege to it. They built siegeworks all around it.

**5** So the city was besieged till the eleventh year of King Zedekiah.

**6** On the ninth day of the fourth month the famine was so severe in the city that there was no food for the people of the land.

**7** Then a breach was made in the city, and all the soldiers fled and went out from the city by night by the way of the gate between the two walls, by the king's garden, though the Chaldeans were all around the city. And they went in the direction of the Arabah.

**8** But the army of the Chaldeans pursued the king and overtook Zedekiah in the plains of Jericho, and all his army was scattered from him.

**9** Then they captured the king and brought him up to the king of Babylon at Riblah in the land of Hamath, and he passed sentence on him.

**10** The king of Babylon killed the sons of Zedekiah before his eyes and also killed all the officials of Judah at Riblah.

**11** He put out the eyes of Zedekiah, and bound him in chains, and the king of Babylon took him to Babylon and put him in prison till the day of his death.

**12** In the fifth month, on the tenth day of the month (it was the nineteenth year of King Nebuchadnezzar, king of Babylon), Nebuzaradan the captain of the guard, who served the king of Babylon, entered Jerusalem.

**13** And he burned the house of the LORD and the king's house and all the houses of Jerusalem. Every great house he burned down.

**14** And all the army of the Chaldeans who were with the captain of the guard broke down all the walls around Jerusalem.

**15** Then Nebuzaradan the captain of the guard carried into exile some of the poorest of the people and the rest of the people who were left in the city and the deserters who had deserted to the king of Babylon, and the rest of the artisans.

**16** But Nebuzaradan the captain of the guard left some of the poorest of the land to be vinedressers and ploughmen.

**17** And the pillars of bronze that were in the house of the LORD, and the stands and the bronze sea that were in the house of the LORD, the Chaldeans broke in pieces, and carried all the bronze to Babylon.

**18** And they took away the pots and the shovels and the snuffers and the basins and the dishes for incense and all the vessels of bronze used in the service,

**19** also the small bowls and the firepans and the basins and the pots and the lampstands and the dishes for incense and the bowls for drink offerings. What was of gold the captain of the guard took away as gold, and what was of silver, as silver.

**20** As for the two pillars, the one sea, the twelve bronze bulls that were under the sea, and the stands, which King Solomon had made for the house of the LORD, the bronze of all these vessels was beyond weight.

**21** As for the pillars, the height of the one pillar was eighteen cubits, and its circumference was twelve cubits. It was four fingers thick, and hollow.

**22** On it was a capital of bronze. The height of the one capital was five cubits. Around the capital all around were network and pomegranates, all of bronze. And the second pillar had the same, with pomegranates.

**23** There were ninety-six pomegranates on the sides. All the pomegranates were a hundred on the network all around.

**24** And the captain of the guard took Seraiah the chief priest, and Zephaniah the second priest, and the three keepers of the threshold,

**25** and from the city he took an officer who had been in command of the soldiers, and seven of the king's counsellors who were found in the city, and the secretary of the commander of the army who mustered the people of the land, and sixty of the people of the land who were found in the midst of the city.

**26** And Nebuzaradan the captain of the guard took them and brought them to the king of Babylon at Riblah.

**27** And the king of Babylon struck them down and put them to death at Riblah in the land of Hamath. So Judah was taken into exile out of its land.

**28** This is the number of the people whom Nebuchadnezzar carried away captive: in the seventh year, 3,023 of the people of Judah.

**29** In the eighteenth year of Nebuchadnezzar he carried away captive from Jerusalem 832 persons.

**30** In the twenty-third year of Nebuchadnezzar, Nebuzaradan the captain of the guard carried away captive of the people of Judah 745 persons. All the persons were 4,600.

**31** And in the thirty-seventh year of the exile of Jehoiachin king of Judah, in the twelfth month, on the twenty-fifth day of the month, Evil-merodach king of Babylon, in the year he began to reign, graciously freed Jehoiachin king of Judah and brought him out of prison.

**32** And he spoke kindly to him and gave him a seat above the seats of the kings who were with him in Babylon.

**33** So Jehoiachin put off his prison garments, and every day of his life he dined regularly at the king's table.

**34** And for his allowance, a regular allowance was given him by the king, according to his daily needs, until the day of his death, as long as he lived.

*Review note: Jeremiah 52 was drafted in WABV wording. It is the last chapter of the book. It is an account of the reign and the fall of Zedekiah (52:1-11), the burning of the city and the temple and the exile (52:12-30), and the release of Jehoiachin in Babylon (52:31-34). It is in prose, and it is not in the form of the words of Jeremiah: the last words of chapter 51 are "Thus far are the words of Jeremiah", and chapter 52 is an addition. The chapter is close to 2 Kings 24:18 to 25:30, and in places it is the same, and the footnotes record the verses. The wording is open to be matched word for word with the WABV 2 Kings when its link is given, and the verses 52:1-3 (2 Kings 24:18-20), 52:4-16 (2 Kings 25:1-12), 52:17-23 (2 Kings 25:13-17), 52:24-27 (2 Kings 25:18-21) and 52:31-34 (2 Kings 25:27-30) are the verses to be matched. The verses 52:28-30 are not in 2 Kings and are not in the Greek, and the footnote records the matter. The verses 52:4-16 are also close to 39:1-10, and the footnote records the differences. In 52:1 the age of Zedekiah (twenty-one years) and the name of his mother are as in 2 Kings 24:18, and the footnote records that the same name, Hamutal, is the name of the mother of Jehoahaz (2 Kings 23:31). In 52:11 the putting out of the eyes of Zedekiah, in 52:10 the killing of his sons and in 52:13 the burning of the house of the LORD are rendered as written, and in 52:31 "graciously freed" translates the Hebrew *nasa et-rosh*, "lifted up the head", and the footnote records the idiom. There are no tagged words in chapter 52. There are no departures from the Hebrew in chapter 52. The Hebrew is hard in some places, including 52:3, 52:15, 52:17-23, 52:25 and 52:28-30. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Appendix A: Footnotes

- **1:1** "Jeremiah" translates *Yirmeyahu*. "Anathoth" is a town of the priests in Benjamin, a short way from Jerusalem (see 1 Kings 2:26).
- **1:2** "The thirteenth year of his reign" of Josiah is 627 before Christ.
- **1:3** "The eleventh year of Zedekiah" is 587 or 586 before Christ, the year in which Jerusalem fell.
- **1:5** "I formed you" translates *etsorkha*, "I knew you" translates *yeda'tikha*, and "I consecrated you" translates *hiqdashtikha*. See entry H-QADOSH. "A prophet" translates *navi*.
- **1:6** "A boy" translates *na'ar*, which means a young person and is not a statement of an exact age.
- **1:10** "To pluck up and to break down, to destroy and to overthrow, to build and to plant" translates six verbs (*linto'ts ve-lintots ve-le'abbid ve-laharos li-vnot ve-linto'a*). The same verbs are used in 18:7-9, 24:6, 31:28 and 42:10.
- **1:11** "A branch of an almond tree" translates *maqqel shaqed*, and "watching" in verse 12 translates *shoqed*, in a play on words that the English cannot keep.
- **1:13** "A boiling pot" translates *sir nafuach*, and "facing away from the north" translates *u-fanav mi-penei tsafonah*, which is hard and may be read "tilted from the north".
- **1:17** "Dress yourself for work" translates *te'zor motnekha*, "gird up your loins".
- **2:2** "The steadfast love of your youth" translates *chesed ne'urayikh*. See entry H-HESED. "Your love as a bride" translates *ahavat kelulotayikh*.
- **2:3** "Holy" translates *qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The firstfruits of his harvest" translates *reshit tevu'atoh*, and "incurred guilt" translates *ye'shamu*.
- **2:5** "Worthlessness" translates *ha-hevel*, and "became worthless" translates *va-yehbalu*. The word *hevel* is the word of Ecclesiastes 1:2 ("breath"), and the team's decision on that word (see Part 20 of the review package) applies here.
- **2:8** "The teaching" translates *ha-torah*. See entry H-TORAH. "The shepherds" translates *ha-ro'im*.
- **2:10** "The coasts of Cyprus" translates *iyyei Kittim*, and "Kedar" is the desert tribes of the east.
- **2:13** "The fountain of living waters" translates *meqor mayim chayyim*, and "cisterns" translates *be'erot*. See also John 4:10.
- **2:16** "Memphis" translates *Nof*, and "Tahpanhes" translates *Tachpanches*, both cities of Egypt. "Have broken the crown of your head" translates *yir'ukha qodqod*, which is hard and may be read "have shaved" or "have grazed on".
- **2:18** "The Nile" translates *Shichor*.
- **2:20** "You broke your yoke" follows the reading tradition and the versions (*shavarti*, with the second person). The written text has the first person. "I will not serve" follows the reading tradition (*e'evod*), and the written text has "I will not transgress" (*e'evor*). "A prostitute" translates *zonah*.
- **2:21** "A choice vine" translates *soreq*, "degenerate" translates *sorriyyot*, and "a wild vine" translates *gefen nokhriyyah*.
- **2:22** "Lye" translates *neter*, and "soap" translates *borit*.
- **2:23** "The Baals" translates *ha-Be'alim*, and "a restless young camel" translates *bikrah qallah meshare'ket*.
- **2:24** "A wild donkey" translates *pere*. "Sniffing the wind" translates *sha'afah ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "Lust" translates *ta'avat nafsha*, literally "the desire of her soul" (see entry H-NEPHESH, which is not tagged in the text here). "In her month" translates *be-chodshah*.
- **2:25** "Unshod" translates *yachef*, and "it is hopeless" translates *nho'ash*.
- **2:34** "The lifeblood of the guiltless poor" translates *dam nafshot evyonim neqiyyim*, literally "the blood of the souls of the guiltless poor". "Breaking in" translates *machteret*. The verse is hard.
- **2:36** "Changing your way" translates *le-shanot et-darkekh*.
- **3:1** The law of divorce is as in Deuteronomy 24:1-4. "Would you return to me" translates *ve-shov elai*, which may be read as a question (as here) or as a command ("yet return to me"). "Prostitute" translates *zanit*.
- **3:2** "The bare heights" translates *shefayim*, "like a nomad" translates *ke-Aravi*, and "been lain with" translates *shugga'at*, a reading that the tradition softens to a gentler word.
- **3:3** "Showers" translates *revivim*, "the spring rain" translates *malqosh*, and "forehead" translates *metsach*.
- **3:4** "The friend" translates *alluf*.
- **3:6** "Faithless" translates *meshuvah*, from the root *shuv*, "turn" or "return". The word has the sense "turning away" and sounds like the verb "return". See entry H-SHUV. The word is used of Israel in 3:6, 3:8, 3:11 and 3:12, of the sons in 3:14 and 3:22, and in 3:22 in the plural (*meshuvoteikhem*). The WABV text keeps the same English word each time, so that the play on words is kept.
- **3:7** "Treacherous" translates *bogedah*.
- **3:8** "A decree of divorce" translates *sefer keritutah*.
- **3:9** "Because she took her prostitution lightly" translates *mi-qol zenutah*, which is hard and may be read "from the sound of her prostitution".
- **3:12** "Faithful" translates *chasid*, the adjective of *chesed*. See entry H-HESED.
- **3:14** "Your master" translates *ba'alti bakhem*, which may also be read "I am your husband".
- **3:15** "Shepherds after my own heart" translates *ro'im ke-libbi*.
- **3:16** "The ark of the covenant of the LORD" translates *aron berit YHWH*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **3:19** "A pleasant land" translates *erets chemdah*, and "most beautiful of all nations" translates *tsevi tsivot goyim*, which is hard.
- **3:21** "Pleading" translates *tachanunei*.
- **3:23** "The orgies on the mountains" translates *hamon harim*, which is hard and may be read "the noise on the mountains".
- **3:24** "The shameful thing" translates *ha-bosheth*, a name for Baal.
- **4:1** "Detestable things" translates *shiqqutsim*.
- **4:3** "Fallow ground" translates *nir*, and "thorns" translates *qotsim*. See also Hosea 10:12.
- **4:4** "The foreskin of your hearts" translates *orlot levavkhem*. See also Deuteronomy 10:16 and 30:6.
- **4:5** "The trumpet" translates *shofar*.
- **4:6** "A standard" translates *nes*.
- **4:7** "A lion" translates *aryeh*, and "a destroyer of nations" translates *mashchit goyim*.
- **4:10** "You have utterly deceived" translates *hassei hissetta*. "It will be well with you" translates *shalom yihyeh lakhem*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Their very life" translates *ad-ha-nafesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH. The speaker of the verse may be the prophet, who speaks of the words of the false prophets.
- **4:11** "A hot wind" translates *ruach tsach*. See entry H-RUACH. "To winnow" translates *lizrot*.
- **4:12** "A wind too full for this" translates *ruach male me-elleh*, which is hard.
- **4:13** "The whirlwind" translates *suphah*.
- **4:15** "Dan" and "Mount Ephraim" are the northern edge and the hills of the north.
- **4:16** "Besiegers" translates *notserim*, a rare word.
- **4:19** "The walls of my heart" translates *qirot libbi*, and "my heart is beating wildly" translates *homeh li libbi*. "The alarm of war" translates *teru'at milchamah*.
- **4:22** "Foolish" translates *evil*, and "stupid" translates *sekhalim*.
- **4:23** "Waste and void" translates *tohu va-vohu*, the same words as in Genesis 1:2.
- **4:27** "A full end" translates *kalah*.
- **4:28** "I have not relented" translates *lo nichamti*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **4:30** "The desolate one" translates *shadud*, "scarlet" translates *shani*, and "paint" translates *puk*. "Your lovers" translates *ogevim*, a rare word. "They seek your life" translates *nafshekh yevaqqeshu*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **4:31** "Gasping for breath" translates *tityappeach*.
- **5:1** "A person" translates *ish*, "justice" translates *mishpat*, and "truth" translates *emunah*.
- **5:3** "Truth" translates *emunah*. "Anguish" translates *chalu*, and "correction" translates *musar*. "To repent" translates *la-shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **5:6** "A wolf from the desert" translates *ze'ev aravot*, and "a leopard" translates *namer*.
- **5:7** "Trooped" translates *yitgodadu*, in which the verb may mean "gashed themselves".
- **5:8** "Lusty stallions" translates *susim mezunim*, which is hard.
- **5:10** "Her vine rows" translates *shorotheha*, and "her branches" translates *netishotheha*. The words are rare.
- **5:13** "Wind" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "The word is not in them" translates *ve-ha-dibber ein bahem*, which is hard.
- **5:15** "An enduring nation" translates *goy eitan*, and "an ancient nation" translates *goy me-olam*.
- **5:16** "An open tomb" translates *qever patuach*.
- **5:22** "The sand as the boundary for the sea" translates *chol gevul la-yam*.
- **5:24** "The autumn rain and the spring rain" translates *yoreh u-malqosh*, and "the weeks appointed for the harvest" translates *shavu'ot chuqqot qatsir*.
- **5:26** "They lurk like fowlers" translates *yashur ke-shokh yoqshim*, which is hard.
- **5:28** "Grown sleek" translates *ashetu*, a rare word. "They know no bounds in deeds of evil" translates *gam-avru divrei-ra*, which is hard.
- **5:31** "Rule at their direction" translates *yirdu al-yedeihem*, which is hard and may be read "rule by their authority".
- **6:1** "Tekoa" and "Beth-haccerem" are places south of Jerusalem.
- **6:4** "Let us attack at noon" translates *ve-na'aleh va-tsohorayim*.
- **6:7** "Keeps fresh" translates *heqir*, and "a well" translates *bor*.
- **6:9** "They will glean thoroughly" translates *ola'lu*, which is a command or a statement.
- **6:10** "Uncircumcised" translates *arelah*.
- **6:14** "Peace, peace" translates *shalom shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. The verse is as in 8:11. "Lightly" translates *al-nequllah*.
- **6:16** "The ancient paths" translates *netivot olam*, "the good way" translates *derekh ha-tov*, and "rest for your souls" translates *marge'a le-nafshekhem*. See entry H-NEPHESH. See also Matthew 11:29.
- **6:19** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **6:20** "Frankincense" translates *levonah*, and "sweet cane" translates *qaneh ha-tov*. "Burnt offerings" translates *olotekhem*, and "sacrifices" translates *zivchekhem*.
- **6:21** "Stumbling blocks" translates *mikhshelim*.
- **6:26** "An only son" translates *yachid*.
- **6:27** "An assayer" translates *bachon*, and "a tester" translates *mivtsar*, two rare words.
- **6:28** "Slanderers" translates *rakhil*.
- **6:29** "The bellows" translates *mappuach*, "the lead" translates *oferet*, and "the refining" translates *tsaraf*. The verse is hard.
- **6:30** "Rejected silver" translates *kesef nim'as*.
- **7:1** The sermon is dated in 26:1-6, in the beginning of the reign of Jehoiakim (about 609 before Christ), where the same words are told again.
- **7:2** "The gate of the LORD's house" translates *sha'ar beit YHWH*.
- **7:3** "Amend your ways" translates *heitivu darkekhem*. "I will let you live in this place" translates *va-ashakkenah etkhem ba-maqom ha-zeh*, which may also be read "I will dwell with you in this place". The same is in 7:7.
- **7:4** "Deceptive words" translates *divrei ha-sheqer*. "The temple of the LORD" translates *heikhal YHWH*, and is given three times as the Hebrew has it.
- **7:6** "The foreigner" translates *ger*.
- **7:9** "Will you steal, murder, commit adultery, swear falsely" translates *ha-genov ratsoach ve-nao'f ve-hishshave'a la-sheqer*. See also Exodus 20:13-16 and Hosea 4:2.
- **7:11** "A den of robbers" translates *me'arat peritsim*, and is quoted in Matthew 21:13, Mark 11:17 and Luke 19:46 in the Greek form.
- **7:12** "Shiloh" is the place of the sanctuary in the time of Eli (see 1 Samuel 1 to 4 and Psalm 78:60).
- **7:16** "Do not pray ... do not intercede" translates *al-titpallel ... ve-al-tifga*. See also 11:14, 14:11 and 15:1.
- **7:18** "The queen of heaven" translates *melekhet ha-shamayim*, and "cakes" translates *kavvanim*. See also 44:17-25.
- **7:21** The verses 21 to 23 are hard. "I did not speak ... or command them concerning burnt offerings and sacrifices" translates *lo-dibbarti ... ve-lo tsivviti ... al-divrei olah va-zavach*, and the words may be read as a denial that the sacrifices were the first command (compare Amos 5:25 and Hosea 6:6).
- **7:23** "I will be your God, and you will be my people" is the covenant formula (see Exodus 6:7 and Leviticus 26:12).
- **7:28** "Discipline" translates *musar*, and "truth" translates *emunah*.
- **7:29** "Cut off your hair" translates *gozzi nizrekh*, in which *nezer* means the long hair of one who is set apart.
- **7:31** "Topheth" translates *Tofet*, and "the Valley of the Son of Hinnom" translates *Gei ben-Hinnom*. It is rendered as written. See also Leviticus 18:21, 2 Kings 23:10, Jeremiah 19:5-6 and 32:35.
- **7:32** "The Valley of Slaughter" translates *Gei ha-haregah*.
- **7:34** "The voice of mirth and the voice of gladness, the voice of the bridegroom and the voice of the bride" is the same in 16:9, 25:10 and 33:11, and the wording is matched.
- **8:1** "The bones ... will be brought out of their tombs" is as in 2 Kings 23:16.
- **8:2** "The host of heaven" translates *tseva ha-shamayim*, and "dung" translates *domen*.
- **8:3** "The remnant" translates *she'erit*.
- **8:4** "If one turns away, does he not return" translates *im-yashuv ve-lo yashuv*, in which the same root *shuv* is used twice. See entry H-SHUV.
- **8:5** "Perpetual backsliding" translates *meshuvah netsachat*, from the same root. "They refuse to return" translates *me'anu lashuv*.
- **8:6** "Relents" translates *nicham*. See entry H-NACHAM. "Plunging headlong" translates *shoteph*.
- **8:7** "The stork" translates *chasidah*, which is related to *chesed* and means "the faithful one". "The turtledove, swallow and crane" translates *tor ve-sus ve-agur*. "The rules of the LORD" translates *mishpat YHWH*.
- **8:8** "The teaching of the LORD" translates *torat YHWH*. See entry H-TORAH. "The lying pen of the scribes" translates *et sheqer sofrim*, which is hard.
- **8:11** "Peace, peace" translates *shalom shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. The verses 11 and 12 are as in 6:14-15.
- **8:13** "When I would gather them" translates *asof asifem*, which is hard in the Hebrew.
- **8:14** "Poisoned water" translates *mei-rosh*.
- **8:16** "Their stallions" translates *abbirav*.
- **8:17** "Serpents" translates *nechashim*, and "adders" translates *tsif'onim*.
- **8:18** "My joy is gone" translates *mavligiti*, a rare word. The verse is hard.
- **8:19** "From the length and breadth of the land" translates *me-erets marchaqqim*. The words that follow are the voices of the people and the LORD, and the footnote records that the speakers are open.
- **8:21** "Is wounded" translates *shavarti*, and "dismay" translates *shammah*.
- **8:22** "Balm" translates *tsori*, "a physician" translates *rofe*, and "healing" translates *arukhah*.
- **9:1** The Hebrew of this verse is 8:23.
- **9:2** "A lodging place for travellers" translates *melon orechim*, and "a company" translates *atseret*.
- **9:3** "They bend their tongue like a bow" translates *va-yidreku et-leshonam qishtam sheqer*.
- **9:4** "A slanderer" translates *rakhil*.
- **9:6** "Heaping oppression on oppression, and deceit on deceit" follows a different reading of *shivtekha*. The Hebrew text has *shivtekha be-tokh mirmah*, "your dwelling is in the midst of deceit; through deceit they refuse to know me". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **9:7** "I will refine them" translates *tsorfam*, and "test" translates *bechanti*.
- **9:8** "A deadly arrow" follows the reading tradition (*chets shochet*). The written text has *shachut*, "sharpened".
- **9:10** "Weeping" translates *bekhi*, "a lamentation" translates *qinah*, and "the pastures" translates *ne'ot*.
- **9:11** "A heap of ruins" translates *gallim*, and "a lair of jackals" translates *me'on tannim*.
- **9:13** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **9:15** "Bitter food" translates *la'anah*, and "poisoned water" translates *mei-rosh*.
- **9:17** "The mourning women" translates *ha-meqonenot*, and "the skilful women" translates *ha-chakhamot*.
- **9:19** "How we are ruined" translates *eikh shuddadnu*.
- **9:20** "A lament" translates *nehi*, and "a dirge" translates *qinah*.
- **9:21** "Our windows" translates *challonenu*, and "our palaces" translates *armenoteinu*.
- **9:22** "Sheaves" translates *alummah*, and "the reaper" translates *ha-qotser*.
- **9:24** "Understands and knows me" translates *haskel ve-yado'a oti*. "Steadfast love" translates *chesed*. See entry H-HESED. "Righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verses 23 and 24 are echoed in 1 Corinthians 1:31 and 2 Corinthians 10:17.
- **9:25** "Circumcised merely in the flesh" translates *mulim be-orlah*, which is hard.
- **9:26** "Who cut the corners of their hair" translates *qetsutsei pe'ah*. See also Leviticus 19:27. "Uncircumcised in heart" translates *arelei lev*.
- **10:2** "The signs of the heavens" translates *otot ha-shamayim*.
- **10:3** "The customs" translates *chuqqot*, and "worthless" translates *hevel*. See the footnote on 2:5.
- **10:5** "Scarecrows in a cucumber field" translates *ke-tomer miqshah*, in which *tomer* may mean "a pillar" or "a palm".
- **10:7** "Your due" translates *ki lekha ya'atah*, which is hard.
- **10:8** "They are both stupid and foolish" translates *be-achat yiv'aru ve-yikhsalu*, and "the instruction of idols is but wood" translates *musar havalim etz hu*, which is hard.
- **10:9** "Uphaz" translates *Ufaz*, a place that is not known. "Violet and purple" translates *tekhelet ve-argaman*.
- **10:10** "The true God" translates *Elohim emet*, "the living God" translates *Elohim chayyim*, and "the everlasting King" translates *melekh olam*.
- **10:11** The verse is in Aramaic in the Hebrew text, and it is the only verse of the book in that language. It is kept in its place.
- **10:12** The verses 12 to 16 are as in 51:15-19, and the wording is open to be matched.
- **10:13** "A tumult of waters" translates *hamon mayim*, "the mist" translates *nesi'im*, and "his storehouses" translates *otserotav*. "The wind" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **10:14** "Stupid" translates *nivar*. "Breath" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **10:16** "The portion of Jacob" translates *cheleq Ya'aqov*, and "the tribe" translates *shevet*.
- **10:17** "Your bundle" translates *kin'atekh*, a rare word.
- **10:18** "Slinging out" translates *qole'a*.
- **10:19** "My hurt" translates *shivri*.
- **10:23** "The way of human beings" translates *derekh adam*.
- **10:24** "Correct me" translates *yassereni*.
- **10:25** "Pour out your wrath" translates *shefokh chamatekha*. The verse is nearly the same as Psalm 79:6-7, and the wording is open to be matched.
- **11:2** "This covenant" translates *ha-berit ha-zot*. See entry H-BERIT. It is the covenant of the exodus and of Sinai, with the words of Deuteronomy.
- **11:3** "Cursed" translates *arur*. See also Deuteronomy 27:26.
- **11:4** "The iron furnace" translates *kur ha-barzel*, as in Deuteronomy 4:20. "You will be my people, and I will be your God" is the covenant formula, as in 7:23.
- **11:5** "A land flowing with milk and honey" translates *erets zavat chalav u-devash*. "So be it" translates *amen*.
- **11:9** "A conspiracy" translates *qesher*.
- **11:13** "Shame" translates *bosheth*, a name for Baal (see 3:24).
- **11:14** The verse is as in 7:16, and the wording is matched.
- **11:15** "My beloved" translates *yedidi*, "vile deeds" translates *mezimmot*, and "sacrificial flesh" translates *besar-qodesh*, literally "holy flesh". The verse is hard.
- **11:16** "A green olive tree" translates *zayit ra'anan*, and "a great tumult" translates *hamullah gedolah*.
- **11:18** "Made it known to me" translates *hodi'ani*.
- **11:19** "A gentle lamb" translates *keves alluf*, and "the tree with its fruit" translates *ha-ets be-lachmo*, literally "the tree with its bread", which is hard.
- **11:20** "Who judges righteously" translates *shofet tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The heart and the mind" translates *kelayot va-lev*, literally "kidneys and heart".
- **11:21** "Seek your life" translates *mevaqshim et-nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **12:1** "You are righteous" translates *tsaddiq attah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "I would plead my case" translates *akh mishpatim adabber otakh*, and "treacherous" translates *bogedei vagad*.
- **12:3** "Set them apart" translates *haqdishem*, from the root *qadash*. See entry H-QADOSH. It is not tagged in the text here.
- **12:4** "He will not see our latter end" translates *lo yir'eh et-acharitenu*. The Greek has "God will not see our ways".
- **12:5** "The thicket of the Jordan" translates *ge'on ha-Yarden*.
- **12:7** "The beloved of my soul" translates *yedidut nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **12:9** "A hyena's lair" translates *ayit tsavua*, which is hard.
- **12:12** "The bare heights" translates *shefayim*. "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **12:15** "I will again have compassion on them" translates *richamtim*, from *racham*.
- **13:1** "A linen waistcloth" translates *ezor pishtim*.
- **13:4** "The Euphrates" translates *Perat*, which may also be a place near Anathoth (compare *Parah* in Joshua 18:23). The same is in 13:5-7.
- **13:9** "The pride" translates *ge'on*.
- **13:11** "A people, a name, a praise, and a glory" translates *le-am u-le-shem u-le-tehillah u-le-tiferet*.
- **13:12** "A jar" translates *nevel*.
- **13:16** "The twilight mountains" translates *harei nashef*.
- **13:17** "My soul" translates *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **13:18** "The queen mother" translates *gevirah*.
- **13:19** "Wholly taken into exile" translates *galut shelomim*, in which *shelomim* means "in full" and is not tagged.
- **13:22** "Your skirts are lifted up and you suffer violence" translates *niglu shulayikh nechmesu aqevayikh*, literally "your heels suffer violence", which is hard.
- **13:23** "The Cushite" translates *Kushi*, and "the leopard" translates *namer*.
- **13:24** "Chaff" translates *qash*, and "the desert wind" translates *ruach midbar*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **13:27** "Your neighings" translates *mitsholotayikh*, and "lewd" translates *zimmat*.
- **14:1** "The drought" translates *batstsarot*.
- **14:3** "Cisterns" translates *gevim*.
- **14:6** "They pant for air like jackals" translates *sha'afu ruach ka-tannim*, in which *ruach* means "air" or "breath" and is not tagged.
- **14:7** "Backslidings" translates *meshuvoteinu*, from *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **14:8** "Hope" translates *miqveh*, and "saviour" translates *moshio*.
- **14:9** "Confused" translates *nidham*.
- **14:11** "The good of this people" translates *le-tovah*, and is not *shalom*.
- **14:12** "Burnt offering and grain offering" translates *olah u-minchah*.
- **14:13** "Assured peace" translates *shalom emet*, literally "peace of truth". See entry H-SHALOM.
- **14:14** "A lying vision, worthless divination" translates *chazon sheqer ve-qesem ve-elil*.
- **14:17** "The young daughter of my people" translates *betulat bat-ammi*.
- **14:18** "Ply their trade" translates *saharu*, which is hard.
- **14:19** "Your soul loathe" translates *ga'alah nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Peace" translates *shalom*, and "healing" translates *marpe*. The verse is as in 8:15.
- **14:21** "Do not spurn" translates *al-tin'ats*, and "your glorious throne" translates *kisse kevodekha*. "Your covenant" translates *beritekha*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **14:22** "The false gods of the nations" translates *havlei ha-goyim*, in which the word *hevel* is the same as in 2:5 and 10:3.
- **15:1** "Moses and Samuel" are the two who prayed for the people (see Exodus 32:11-14, 1 Samuel 7:9 and 12:23, and Psalm 99:6).
- **15:2** "Pestilence" translates *mavet*, and "captivity" translates *shevi*.
- **15:3** "Four kinds of destroyers" translates *arba mishpachot*.
- **15:4** "Manasseh" is the king of 2 Kings 21:1-16 and 23:26-27.
- **15:5** "Your welfare" translates *le-shalom lakh*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **15:6** "I am weary of relenting" translates *nil'eiti hinnachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **15:7** "I have winnowed them" translates *va-azrem*, and "they did not turn" translates *lo-shavu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **15:8** "A destroyer" translates *shoded*.
- **15:9** "She who bore seven" translates *yoledet ha-shiv'ah*.
- **15:10** "A person of strife and contention" translates *ish riv ve-ish madon*.
- **15:11** "I will set you free" translates *sheritikha*, which is hard and may be read "I will strengthen you".
- **15:13** The verses 13 and 14 are close to 17:3-4, and the Hebrew differs in a few words.
- **15:15** "Visit me" translates *pokdeni*, "your forbearance" translates *erekh appekha*, and "reproach" translates *cherpah*.
- **15:16** "Your words were found, and I ate them" translates *nimtse'u devarekha va-okhelem*, and "the delight of my heart" translates *sason u-lesimchat levavi*.
- **15:17** "Revellers" translates *mesachaqim*, and "indignation" translates *za'am*.
- **15:18** "A deceitful brook" translates *akhzav*, a stream that dries in summer.
- **15:19** "If you return" translates *im-tashuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "What is precious" translates *yaqar*, and "what is worthless" translates *zollel*.
- **15:20** "A fortified wall of bronze" translates *chomat nechoshet betsurah*, as in 1:18.
- **15:21** "Redeem" translates *padah*, "ransom", and is not tagged.
- **16:2** "A wife" translates *ishah*.
- **16:4** "Deadly diseases" translates *mamotei tachalu'im*.
- **16:5** "The house of mourning" translates *beit marzeach*. "My peace" translates *shelomi*. See entry H-SHALOM. "My steadfast love and my mercy" translates *ha-chesed ve-ha-rachamim*. See entry H-HESED.
- **16:7** "To comfort" translates *le-nachamo*. See entry H-NACHAM. "The cup of consolation" translates *kos ha-tanchumim*.
- **16:9** The verse is as in 7:34, and the wording is matched.
- **16:11** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **16:12** "His stubborn evil will" translates *sherirut libbo ha-ra*.
- **16:14** The verses 14 and 15 are as in 23:7-8, and the wording is matched.
- **16:16** "Fishers" translates *dayyagim*, and "hunters" translates *tsayyadim*.
- **16:18** "Doubly" translates *mishneh*, "the carcasses" translates *nivlat*, and "their detestable idols" translates *shiqqutseihem*.
- **16:21** "This once" translates *pa'am achat*.
- **17:1** "A pen of iron" translates *et barzel*, and "a point of diamond" translates *tsiporen shamir*. "The tablet of their heart" translates *luach libbam*.
- **17:2** "While their children remember their altars" translates *ke-zekor beneihem mizbechotam*, which is hard.
- **17:3** "Your high places for sin" translates *bamoteikha be-chatta*, which is hard. The verses 3 and 4 are close to 15:13-14.
- **17:4** "You will loose your hand" translates *ve-shamatta u-vekha*, which is hard.
- **17:5** "The one who trusts in human beings" translates *ha-gever asher yivtach ba-adam*, and "flesh" translates *basar*.
- **17:6** "A shrub" translates *ar'ar*, and "a salt land" translates *erets melechah*.
- **17:7** The verses 7 and 8 are close to Psalm 1:3, and the wording is open to be matched.
- **17:9** "Deceitful" translates *aqov*, and "desperately sick" translates *anush*.
- **17:11** "The partridge" translates *qore*.
- **17:12** The verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **17:13** "The hope of Israel" translates *miqveh Yisra'el*. "Will be written in the earth" translates *ba-arets yikkatevu*, which is hard.
- **17:14** "My praise" translates *tehillati*.
- **17:16** "I have not run away from being your shepherd" translates *lo-atsti me-ro'eh acharekha*, which is hard.
- **17:18** "Double destruction" translates *mishneh shibbaron*.
- **17:19** "The People's Gate" translates *sha'ar bnei-ha-am*, which is not known.
- **17:21** "For the sake of your lives" translates *be-nafshotekhem*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "A burden" translates *massa*.
- **17:22** "Keep the Sabbath day holy" translates *qaddeshu et-yom ha-shabbat*. See entry H-QADOSH. See also Exodus 20:8.
- **17:23** "Instruction" translates *musar*.
- **17:26** "The Shephelah" is the lowland of the west, and "the Negeb" is the south.
- **18:2** "The potter" translates *yotser*.
- **18:3** "His wheel" translates *ha-ovnayim*, literally "the two stones".
- **18:4** "Was spoiled" translates *nishchat*.
- **18:7** The six verbs (*linto'ts ve-linttots ve-leha'avid* and in verse 9 *libnot ve-linto'a*) are as in 1:10 and 31:28.
- **18:8** "Turns" translates *shav*. See entry H-SHUV. "I will relent" translates *nichamti*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **18:11** "I am shaping disaster against you" translates *yotser alekhem ra'ah*, in which the verb *yotser* is the word "potter", so that the play on words is kept in the Hebrew.
- **18:12** "It is hopeless" translates *nio'ash*.
- **18:13** "The young woman Israel" translates *betulat Yisra'el*. The rendering of *betulah* is open to the team's decision (see Part 22 of the review package).
- **18:14** "The snow of Lebanon leave the crags of Sirion" is hard in the Hebrew, and the words *tsur sadai* are uncertain.
- **18:15** "False gods" translates *la-shav*, and "the ancient roads" translates *shevilei olam*.
- **18:16** "A thing to be hissed at" translates *sheriqot*.
- **18:17** "The east wind" translates *ruach qadim*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **18:18** "The teaching" translates *torah*. See entry H-TORAH. "Let us strike him with the tongue" translates *nakkehu va-lashon*.
- **18:20** "For my life" translates *le-nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **18:21** "Give them over to the power of the sword" translates *haggeram al-yedei cherev*.
- **18:23** "Blot out" translates *tim'chi*.
- **19:1** "An earthenware flask" translates *baqbuq*, and the word has the sound of the verb "I will make void" (*baqqoti*) in verse 7.
- **19:2** "The Potsherd Gate" translates *sha'ar ha-charsit*, which is hard.
- **19:3** "Will tingle" translates *titsallena*.
- **19:5** The verse is as in 7:31, and the wording is matched.
- **19:6** The verse is as in 7:32.
- **19:9** "I will make them eat the flesh of their sons and the flesh of their daughters" is rendered as written. See also Leviticus 26:29, Deuteronomy 28:53 and Lamentations 4:10.
- **19:13** "Defiled" translates *temei'im*.
- **20:1** "Pashhur" translates *Pashchur*, and "chief officer" translates *nagid*.
- **20:2** "The stocks" translates *ha-mahpekhet*.
- **20:3** "Terror on Every Side" translates *Magor missaviv*. See also 6:25, 20:10, 46:5 and 49:29.
- **20:7** "You have deceived me, and I was deceived" translates *pittitani YHWH va-efat*, in which the verb may also be read "you have enticed me, and I was enticed". "You have prevailed" translates *va-tukhal*.
- **20:9** "A burning fire shut up in my bones" translates *ke-esh boèret atsur be-atsmotai*.
- **20:10** "Whispering" translates *dibbat rabbim*, "denounce" translates *haggidu*, and "watching for my fall" translates *shomrei tsal'i*.
- **20:11** "A dread warrior" translates *gibbor aritz*.
- **20:12** "Who tests the righteous" translates *bochen tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verse is close to 11:20, and the Hebrew differs in a few words.
- **20:13** "The life of the needy" translates *nefesh evyon*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **20:14** The verses 14 to 18 are rendered as written, with no comment. See also Job 3:1-10.
- **20:17** "Her womb for ever great" translates *rachmah harat olam*, which is hard.
- **21:1** Zedekiah was king from 597 to 587 before Christ. "Pashhur the son of Malchijah" is not the Pashhur of 20:1. "Zephaniah the priest" is named again in 29:25, 37:3 and 52:24.
- **21:2** "Nebuchadnezzar" translates *Nevukhadre'tstsar*. "All his wonderful deeds" translates *kekhol-nifle'otav*, a word used of the exodus.
- **21:5** "Outstretched hand and strong arm" translates *be-yad netuyah u-vizro'a chazaqah*, the words of the exodus (Deuteronomy 4:34), here turned against the people.
- **21:7** "Those who seek their lives" translates *mevaqshei nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **21:8** "The way of life and the way of death" translates *derekh ha-chayyim ve-derekh ha-mavet*. See also Deuteronomy 30:15 and 19.
- **21:9** "Surrenders" translates *nafal al*, literally "falls to". "His life as a prize of war" translates *nafsho lo le-shalal*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **21:12** "The oppressor" translates *ashuq*.
- **21:13** "Inhabitant of the valley, rock of the plain" translates *yoshevet ha-emeq tsur ha-mishor*, a name for Jerusalem.
- **21:14** "Her forest" translates *ya'arah*, which may mean the cedar palaces (see 22:7 and 1 Kings 7:2).
- **22:3** "The foreigner" translates *ger*.
- **22:5** "I swear by myself" translates *bi nishba'ti*, as in Genesis 22:16.
- **22:6** "Gilead" and "the summit of Lebanon" are figures of beauty and height.
- **22:7** "Destroyers" translates *mashchitim*.
- **22:9** "The covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **22:10** "The one who is dead" is Josiah, and "the one who goes away" is Shallum, who was taken to Egypt.
- **22:11** "Shallum" is Jehoahaz, who reigned three months in 609 before Christ (see 2 Kings 23:30-34).
- **22:13** "His upper rooms" translates *aliyyotav*.
- **22:14** "Vermilion" translates *shashar*.
- **22:15** "Because you compete in cedar" translates *ki-attah mitchareh ba-arez*, which is hard.
- **22:16** "He judged the cause" translates *din din*, and "to know me" translates *ha-da'at oti*.
- **22:17** "Dishonest gain" translates *betsa*.
- **22:18** "Jehoiakim" reigned from 609 to 598 before Christ. "Ah, my brother" translates *hoy achi*, and "his majesty" translates *hodoh*.
- **22:19** "The burial of a donkey" translates *qevurat chamor*. It is rendered as written. See also 36:30.
- **22:20** "Abarim" is the mountains east of the Dead Sea.
- **22:21** "Your prosperity" translates *shalvotayikh*, from *shalvah*, which means ease, and is not *shalom*.
- **22:22** "The wind will shepherd all your shepherds" translates *ro'ah kol-ro'ayikh tir'eh-ruach*, in which *ruach* means "wind". See entry H-RUACH.
- **22:23** "Nested" translates *megunenet*, and "how you will be pitied" translates *mah-nechant*, which is hard.
- **22:24** "Coniah" translates *Konyahu*, who is Jeconiah or Jehoiachin (see 2 Kings 24:6-17). He reigned three months in 597 before Christ. "The signet ring" translates *chotam*. See also Haggai 2:23.
- **22:25** "Those who seek your life" translates *mevaqshei nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **22:26** "The mother who bore you" is Nehushta (see 2 Kings 24:8).
- **22:28** "A despised, broken pot" translates *atsev nivzeh nafots*.
- **22:29** "Land, land, land" translates *erets erets erets*.
- **22:30** "Childless" translates *ariri*. See also Matthew 1:11-12.
- **23:1** "The shepherds" translates *ro'im*, a word for kings and leaders.
- **23:2** "You have not attended to them" translates *lo pequdtem otam*, and "I will attend to you" translates *poqed alekhem*, in a play on the same verb.
- **23:3** "Be fruitful and multiply" translates *parah ve-ravah*, as in Genesis 1:28.
- **23:5** "A righteous Branch" translates *tsemach tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. See also 33:15, Isaiah 4:2, 11:1, Zechariah 3:8 and 6:12.
- **23:6** "The LORD is our righteousness" translates *YHWH tsidqenu*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The name has a likeness to the name of Zedekiah (*Tsidqiyyahu*, "the LORD is my righteousness").
- **23:7** The verses 7 and 8 are as in 16:14-15, and the wording is matched.
- **23:9** "My heart is broken" translates *nishbar libbi*, and "a drunken person" translates *ke-ish shikkor*. "His holy words" translates *divrei qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **23:10** "The curse" translates *alah*, and "their might is not right" translates *u-gevuratam lo-khen*.
- **23:11** "Ungodly" translates *chanefu*.
- **23:13** "An unsavoury thing" translates *tifla*.
- **23:14** "A horrible thing" translates *sha'arurah*. "Turns" translates *shav*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **23:16** "Filling you with vain hopes" translates *mahbilim hemmah etkhem*, from *hevel*.
- **23:17** "It will be well with you" translates *shalom yihyeh lakhem*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **23:18** "The council" translates *sod*.
- **23:19** The verses 19 and 20 are close to 30:23-24, and the wording is open to be matched.
- **23:23** "A God at hand" translates *Elohei miqqarov*.
- **23:25** "I have dreamed" translates *chalamti*.
- **23:28** "Straw" translates *teven*, and "wheat" translates *bar*.
- **23:29** "A hammer" translates *pattish*.
- **23:31** "Who use their tongues and declare" translates *ha-loqechim leshonam va-yin'u ne'um*.
- **23:33** "You are the burden" follows the Greek and the Latin. The Hebrew text has *et-mah-massa*, "what burden?". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar. The word *massa* means "burden" and "oracle", and the play on the two senses is kept as "burden" in 23:33-40. In the headings of Isaiah 13 to 23 it is "oracle".
- **23:36** "You pervert" translates *haphakhtem*.
- **23:39** "I will surely lift you up" follows some Hebrew manuscripts and the versions (*nasho essa*). The Hebrew text has *nasho nashiti*, "I will surely forget you". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **23:40** "Everlasting reproach and perpetual shame" translates *cherpat olam u-khlimmut olam*.
- **24:1** "Jeconiah" translates *Yekhonyahu*, who was taken to Babylon in 597 before Christ (see 2 Kings 24:10-17). "The metalworkers" translates *ha-masger*, "the smiths". "Baskets of figs" translates *dudei te'enim*.
- **24:2** "First-ripe figs" translates *ke-te'enei ha-bikkurot*.
- **24:5** "I will regard as good" translates *akkir le-tovah*.
- **24:6** "Build ... plant" are the verbs of 1:10, as in 18:9 and 31:28.
- **24:7** "A heart to know that I am the LORD" translates *lev la-da'at oti ki ani YHWH*. See also 31:33-34 and 32:39. "They will return" translates *yashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **24:8** "Those who live in the land of Egypt" are those who fled there after the first exile (see also 43:4-7).
- **24:9** "A horror" translates *za'avah*, and "a byword" translates *mashal*.
- **25:1** "The fourth year of Jehoiakim" is 605 before Christ, the year of the battle of Carchemish (see 46:2).
- **25:3** "Twenty-three years" is from 627 to 605 before Christ. "Persistently" translates *hashkem*.
- **25:5** "Turn" translates *shuvu*. See entry H-SHUV. "From of old and for ever" translates *le-min-olam ve-ad-olam*.
- **25:9** "My servant" translates *avdi*, said of Nebuchadnezzar. "I will devote them to destruction" translates *hacharamtim*. "A hissing" translates *sheriqah*.
- **25:10** The verse is as in 7:34, with the grinding of the millstones (*qol rechayim*) and the light of the lamp (*or ner*) added.
- **25:11** "Seventy years" translates *shiv'im shanah*. See also 29:10, 2 Chronicles 36:21, Daniel 9:2 and Zechariah 1:12.
- **25:12** "An everlasting waste" translates *shimemot olam*.
- **25:13** "Everything written in this book, which Jeremiah prophesied against all the nations" follows the Hebrew text. In the Greek the oracles against the nations stand at this place, and in the Hebrew they are in chapters 46 to 51.
- **25:15** "The cup of the wine of wrath" translates *kos ha-yayin ha-chemah ha-zot*. See also Isaiah 51:17 and Revelation 14:10.
- **25:16** "Stagger" translates *hitgo'ashu*, and "be crazed" translates *hit'halelu*.
- **25:18** "A hissing and a curse" translates *sheriqah ve-qelalah*.
- **25:20** "The mixed tribes" translates *ha-erev*, and "Uz" is a land of the south-east.
- **25:23** "Dedan, Tema, Buz" are places of the Arabian desert. "All who cut the corners of their hair" translates *kol-qetsutsei pe'ah*, as in 9:26.
- **25:25** "Zimri" is not known.
- **25:26** "The king of Sheshach" translates *melekh Sheshakh*. The name *Sheshach* is a coded name for Babylon, in which the letters of *Bavel* are replaced by those in the opposite place in the alphabet (see also 51:41).
- **25:27** "Vomit" translates *qu*.
- **25:29** "The city that is called by my name" is Jerusalem (see also 1 Peter 4:17).
- **25:30** "Will roar" translates *yish'ag*, and "his holy habitation" translates *me-me'on qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Shout" translates *hedad*.
- **25:31** "An indictment" translates *riv*, and "is entering into judgement" translates *nishpat*.
- **25:33** "Dung" translates *domen*, as in 8:2 and 16:4.
- **25:34** "Lords of the flock" translates *addirei ha-tson*, and "like a choice vessel" translates *ke-khli chemdah*. The Greek has "like choice rams".
- **25:37** "The peaceful folds" translates *ne'ot ha-shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **25:38** "The sword of the oppressor" translates *cherev ha-yonah*. The word is as in 46:16 and 50:16, and the Greek has "the sword of the dove".
- **26:1** "The beginning of the reign of Jehoiakim" is 609 or 608 before Christ. The chapter gives the aftermath of the sermon of 7:1-15.
- **26:2** "The court of the LORD's house" translates *chatser beit YHWH*.
- **26:3** "Turn" translates *yashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV. "I may relent" translates *ve-nichamti*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **26:4** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **26:5** "Urgently" translates *hashkem*.
- **26:6** "Shiloh" is as in 7:12-14. "A curse" translates *qelalah*.
- **26:8** "You must die" translates *mot tamut*.
- **26:10** "The New Gate" translates *sha'ar ha-chadash*.
- **26:15** "Innocent blood" translates *dam naqi*.
- **26:18** "Micah of Moresheth" translates *Mikhah ha-Morashti*. The words are as in Micah 3:12, and the wording is open to be matched when Micah is drafted.
- **26:20** "Uriah the son of Shemaiah from Kiriath-jearim" is not named anywhere else.
- **26:22** "Elnathan the son of Achbor" is named again in 36:12 and 36:25.
- **26:24** "Ahikam the son of Shaphan" is named again in 2 Kings 22:12 and 25:22 and in Jeremiah 39:14 and 40:5.
- **27:1** "Zedekiah" follows many Hebrew manuscripts and the Syriac. The Hebrew text has "Jehoiakim", and the verses 3 and 12 and 28:1 name Zedekiah. The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **27:2** "Straps" translates *moserot*, and "yoke-bars" translates *motot*.
- **27:3** "Envoys" translates *mal'akhim*.
- **27:5** "By my great power and my outstretched arm" translates *be-khochi ha-gadol u-vi-zero'i ha-netuyah*.
- **27:6** "My servant" translates *avdi*, as in 25:9.
- **27:7** "And his son and his grandson" is not in the Greek. "Until the time of his own land comes" translates *ad-bo et-artso gam-hu*.
- **27:9** "Your diviners" translates *qosmeikhem*, "your dreamers" translates *chalomoteikhem*, "your fortune-tellers" translates *onenekhem*, and "your sorcerers" translates *kashshafeikhem*.
- **27:16** "The vessels of the LORD's house" are as in 28:3 and 52:17-23. The verses 16 to 22 are shorter in the Greek.
- **27:19** "The pillars, the sea, the stands" translates *ha-ammudim ve-ha-yam ve-ha-mekhonot*. See also 1 Kings 7:15-50 and Jeremiah 52:17.
- **27:22** "The day when I visit them" translates *ad-yom pequdati otam*.
- **28:1** "The fourth year" of Zedekiah is 594 or 593 before Christ. "Hananiah the son of Azzur" is from Gibeon.
- **28:2** "I have broken the yoke" translates *shavarti et-ol*.
- **28:6** "Amen" translates *amen*.
- **28:8** "The prophets who preceded you and me" translates *ha-nevi'im asher hayu le-fanai u-lefanekha min-ha-olam*.
- **28:9** "Prophesies of peace" translates *yinnavei le-shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. See also Deuteronomy 18:21-22.
- **28:10** "The yoke-bars" translates *ha-motah*.
- **28:13** "Bars of iron" translates *motot barzel*.
- **28:15** "Trust in a lie" translates *livtoach al-sheqer*.
- **28:16** "I will remove you" translates *mishallechakha*, and "rebellion" translates *sarah*. See also Deuteronomy 13:5.
- **28:17** "In the seventh month" is two months after the word of 28:1.
- **29:1** "The letter" translates *ha-sefer*, and "the surviving elders" translates *yeter ziqnei*.
- **29:2** "The queen mother" translates *ha-gevirah*, "the eunuchs" translates *ha-sarisim*, and "the craftsmen and the metalworkers" translates *ha-charash ve-ha-masger*, as in 24:1.
- **29:3** "Elasah the son of Shaphan" and "Gemariah the son of Hilkiah" are named in 36:10-12.
- **29:5** The verses 5 and 6 are in contrast with the words of Hananiah in 28:2-4.
- **29:7** "The welfare of the city" translates *shelom ha-ir*, "in its welfare" translates *bi-shlomah*, and "your welfare" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. The same word is used three times.
- **29:10** "When seventy years are completed for Babylon" translates *ki le-fi melot le-Bavel shiv'im shanah*. See also 25:11-12. "I will visit you" translates *efqod etkhem*, and "my promise" translates *devari ha-tov*, literally "my good word".
- **29:11** "Plans" translates *machshevot*, and "welfare and not for evil" translates *shalom ve-lo le-ra'ah*. See entry H-SHALOM. "A future and a hope" translates *acharit ve-tiqvah*, which may also be read "an end and a hope".
- **29:13** The verse is as in Deuteronomy 4:29.
- **29:14** "I will restore your fortunes" translates *ve-shavti et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV. The phrase may also be read "I will turn your captivity".
- **29:16** The verses 16 to 20 are not in the Greek.
- **29:17** "Vile figs" translates *ha-te'enim ha-sho'arim*, as in 24:2 and 24:8.
- **29:18** "A curse, a terror, a hissing, and a reproach" translates *le-alah u-le-shammah u-le-sheriqah u-le-cherpah*.
- **29:21** "Ahab the son of Kolaiah" and "Zedekiah the son of Maaseiah" are not the king.
- **29:22** "Roasted" translates *qalam*.
- **29:23** "An outrageous thing" translates *nevalah*.
- **29:24** "Shemaiah of Nehelam" translates *Shemayahu ha-Nechelami*.
- **29:26** "Madman" translates *meshugga*, "the stocks" translates *mahpekhet*, and "neck irons" translates *tsinoq*. See also 20:2.
- **29:28** "Your exile will be long" translates *arukkah hi*.
- **29:32** "Rebellion" translates *sarah*.
- **30:2** "Write in a book" translates *ketov lekha ba-sefer*. See also 36:2.
- **30:3** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ve-shavti et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV. The phrase may also be read "I will turn the captivity", and it is used again in 30:18, 31:23, 32:44, 33:7, 33:11 and 33:26.
- **30:5** "A cry of panic" translates *qol charadah*. "No peace" translates *ve-ein shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **30:6** "His stomach" translates *chalatsav*, and "deathly pale" translates *yeraqon*.
- **30:7** "A time of distress for Jacob" translates *et tsarah hi le-Ya'aqov*. See also Daniel 12:1.
- **30:8** "Their bonds" translates *moseroteihem*.
- **30:9** "David their king" is as in Hosea 3:5 and Ezekiel 34:23-24.
- **30:10** "Will return" translates *ve-shav*. "Quiet and ease" translates *shaqat ve-sha'anan*. The verses 10 and 11 are as in 46:27-28.
- **30:11** "A full end" translates *kalah*, and "in just measure" translates *la-mishpat*.
- **30:12** "Your hurt" translates *shivrekh*, and "grievous" translates *nechlah*.
- **30:13** "None to uphold your cause" translates *ein din dinekh*, and "no healing" translates *teluppah*. The verse is hard.
- **30:14** "Your lovers" translates *me'ahavayikh*, and "the punishment of a merciless foe" translates *musar akhzari*.
- **30:15** "Flagrant" translates *atsemu*.
- **30:17** "An outcast" translates *nidachah*.
- **30:18** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *shav shevut*. See entry H-SHUV. "Have compassion" translates *richamti*, "mound" translates *tel*, and "the palace" translates *armon*.
- **30:21** "Their prince" translates *addiro*, and "their ruler" translates *moshlo*. "Dare" translates *arav et-libbo*, literally "pledge his heart".
- **30:23** The verses 23 and 24 are as in 23:19-20.
- **31:1** "Clans" translates *mishpechot*.
- **31:2** "The people who survived the sword" translates *am sridei cherev*, and "when Israel sought for rest" translates *halokh le-harigo Yisra'el*, which is hard.
- **31:3** "From far away" translates *me-rachoq*. "An everlasting love" translates *ahavat olam*. "Faithfulness" translates *chased*. See entry H-HESED. "I have continued" translates *mashakhtikh*, literally "I have drawn out".
- **31:4** "Young Israel" translates *betulat Yisra'el*. The rendering of *betulah* is open to the team's decision (see Part 22 of the review package).
- **31:5** "Will enjoy" translates *chillelu*. See also Deuteronomy 20:6.
- **31:6** "Watchmen" translates *notsrim*.
- **31:7** "The chief of the nations" translates *rosh ha-goyim*.
- **31:9** "Pleas for mercy" translates *tachanunim*, "brooks" translates *nachalei mayim*, and "my firstborn" translates *bekhori*. See also Exodus 4:22.
- **31:11** "Has ransomed" translates *padah*. "Has redeemed" translates *ge'alo*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **31:12** "Will be radiant" translates *nahar*, and "their life" translates *nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "A watered garden" translates *gan raveh*, as in Isaiah 58:11.
- **31:13** "I will comfort them" translates *nichamtim*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **31:14** "I will feast the soul" translates *rivveti et-nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **31:15** "Ramah" is a place north of Jerusalem (see 40:1). "Rachel is weeping for her children" translates *Rachel mevakkah al-baneha*, and "she refuses to be comforted" translates *me'anah le-hinnachem*. See entry H-NACHAM. The verse is quoted in Matthew 2:18.
- **31:16** "A reward" translates *sakhar*, and "they will come back" translates *shavu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **31:17** "Hope" translates *tiqvah*.
- **31:18** "Grieving" translates *mitnoded*, and "an untrained calf" translates *egel lo lummad*. "Bring me back" translates *hashivveni*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **31:19** "I had turned away" translates *shuvi*, and "I relented" translates *nichamti*. See entries H-SHUV and H-NACHAM. "I struck my thigh" translates *saphaqti al-yarekh*, a sign of grief.
- **31:20** "My dear son" translates *ben yaqqir li*, "my darling child" translates *yeled sha'ashu'im*, "my heart yearns" translates *hamu me'ai*, and "I will surely have mercy" translates *racheim arachamennu*.
- **31:21** "Road markers" translates *tsiyyunim*, and "guideposts" translates *tamrurim*. "Return" translates *shuvi*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **31:22** "Waver" translates *techammaqin*. "Faithless daughter" translates *ha-bat ha-shovevah*. See entry H-SHUV. "A new thing" translates *chadashah*, and "a woman encircles a man" translates *neqevah tesovev gaver*, which is hard.
- **31:23** "I restore their fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevutam*. See entry H-SHUV. "Habitation of righteousness" translates *nevat tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Holy hill" translates *har ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **31:25** "The weary soul" translates *nefesh ayefah*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "I will replenish" translates *mille'ti*.
- **31:26** "I awoke" translates *hiqitsoti*, and the verse may be the words of the prophet on a dream.
- **31:27** "I will sow" translates *zara'ti*.
- **31:28** "As I have watched over them to pluck up and break down" uses the verbs of 1:10, with *shaqadti* ("I have watched") as in 1:12.
- **31:29** "Sour grapes" translates *boser*. See also Ezekiel 18:2.
- **31:31** "A new covenant" translates *berit chadashah*. See entry H-BERIT. The words "new covenant" are used in the Hebrew Bible here only. The passage 31:31-34 is quoted in Luke 22:20, 1 Corinthians 11:25, 2 Corinthians 3:6 and Hebrews 8:8-12 and 10:16-17, and the footnote does not comment on how it is read.
- **31:32** "They broke" translates *heferu*. "Though I was their husband" translates *ve-anokhi ba'alti vam*, in which the verb may be read "I was their husband", "I was their master" or, as the Greek has it, "I neglected them".
- **31:33** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH. "Within them" translates *be-qirbam*, and "I will write it on their hearts" translates *al-libbam ektavennah*.
- **31:34** "Know the LORD" translates *de'u et-YHWH*, and "I will forgive" translates *eslach*. "I will remember their sin no more" translates *lo ezkor-od*.
- **31:35** "The fixed order" translates *chuqqot*.
- **31:36** "Will cease" translates *yishbetu*.
- **31:38** "The Tower of Hananel" and "the Corner Gate" are in the north of Jerusalem (see Nehemiah 3:1 and Zechariah 14:10).
- **31:39** "Gareb" and "Goah" are not known.
- **31:40** "The valley of the dead bodies and the ashes" translates *emeq ha-pegarim ve-ha-deshen*, and "the Horse Gate" is as in Nehemiah 3:28. "Holy to the LORD" translates *qodesh la-YHWH*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **32:1** "The tenth year of Zedekiah" is 588 or 587 before Christ, the year of the siege, and "the eighteenth year of Nebuchadnezzar" is the same year.
- **32:2** "The court of the guard" translates *chatsar ha-mattarah*.
- **32:3** "Had shut him up" translates *kala'o*.
- **32:4** "Face to face ... eye to eye" translates *peh el-peh ve-eynav et-einav tir'ennah*. See also 34:3 and 39:5-7.
- **32:5** "Until I visit him" translates *ad-pequddi oto*.
- **32:7** "Hanamel" translates *Chanam'el*. "The right of redemption" translates *mishpat ha-ge'ullah*. See entry H-GOEL. See also Leviticus 25:25-28 and Ruth 4:1-6.
- **32:9** "Seventeen shekels of silver" translates *shiv'ah asar ha-kasef*.
- **32:10** "The deed" translates *sefer*, "sealed" translates *chatamti*, and "witnesses" translates *edim*.
- **32:11** "Containing the terms and conditions" translates *ha-mitsvah ve-ha-chuqqim*, which is hard. "The open copy" translates *ha-galui*.
- **32:12** "Baruch the son of Neriah" is named again in 36:4, 43:3 and 45:1.
- **32:14** "An earthenware vessel" translates *keli-cheres*.
- **32:17** "Nothing is too hard for you" translates *lo-yippale mimmekha kol-davar*. The same is in 32:27.
- **32:18** "You show steadfast love to thousands" translates *oseh chesed la-alafim*. See entry H-HESED. "You repay the guilt of fathers to their children" translates *u-meshallem avon avot el-cheiq beneihem*. See also Exodus 20:5-6, 34:6-7 and Deuteronomy 5:9-10.
- **32:20** "Signs and wonders" translates *otot u-mofetim*.
- **32:21** "Great terror" translates *mora gadol*.
- **32:23** "Your teaching" translates *be-toratekha*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **32:24** "The siege mounds" translates *ha-solelot*.
- **32:27** "The God of all flesh" translates *Elohei kol-basar*.
- **32:29** The roofs of the houses are as in 19:13.
- **32:33** "I have taught them persistently" translates *lammed hashkem ve-lammed*, and "to receive instruction" translates *laqachat musar*.
- **32:35** "To offer up their sons and daughters to Molech" translates *le-ha'avir et-beneihem u-venoteihem la-Molekh*. It is rendered as written. See also 7:31, 19:5 and Leviticus 18:21.
- **32:37** "In safety" translates *la-vetach*.
- **32:39** "One heart and one way" translates *lev echad ve-derekh echad*. See also Ezekiel 11:19.
- **32:40** "An everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT. "I will not turn away" translates *lo-ashuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "They may not turn" translates *lo-yasuru*, from *sur*, and is not tagged.
- **32:41** "In faithfulness" translates *be-emet*, and "with all my soul" translates *be-khol-nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **32:44** "I will restore their fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevutam*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **33:2** "Who made the earth" translates *oseh erets*, and "who formed it to establish it" translates *yotser otah le-hakhinah*.
- **33:3** "Great and hidden things" translates *gedolot u-vetsurot*, in which *betsurot* may mean "fortified" or "unreachable".
- **33:4** "Were torn down" translates *hannetutsim*. The verses 4 and 5 are hard.
- **33:5** "They are coming in to fight" translates *ba'im lehillachem*, which is hard.
- **33:6** "Health and healing" translates *arukhah u-marpe*. "Prosperity" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Security" translates *emet*, literally "truth".
- **33:8** "I will cleanse" translates *ve-tiharti*, and "I will forgive" translates *ve-salachti*. "Rebellion" translates *pasha'u*.
- **33:9** "A name of joy" translates *shem sasson*. "Prosperity" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **33:11** "The voice of mirth ... the voice of the bride" is as in 7:34 and 25:10. "Give thanks to the LORD of hosts, for the LORD is good, for his steadfast love endures for ever" is as in Psalm 106:1, 107:1, 118:1 and 136:1 and in Ezra 3:11, and the wording is open to be matched with the Psalms. See entry H-HESED. "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **33:13** "The one who counts them" translates *moneh*.
- **33:14** The verses 14 to 26 are not in the Greek.
- **33:15** "A righteous Branch" translates *tsemach tsedaqah*, "a Branch of righteousness", where 23:5 has *tsemach tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verses 15 and 16 are close to 23:5-6.
- **33:16** "It will be called" translates *asher yiqra-lah*, in which the Hebrew has "it" (of Jerusalem), where 23:6 has "he". "The LORD is our righteousness" translates *YHWH tsidqenu*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **33:17** "David will never lack a man to sit on the throne" is as in 2 Samuel 7:12-16 and 1 Kings 2:4.
- **33:18** "The Levitical priests" translates *ha-kohanim ha-leviyyim*.
- **33:20** "My covenant with the day and my covenant with the night" translates *berit ha-yom u-verit ha-laylah*. See entry H-BERIT. See also Genesis 8:22 and 9:8-17.
- **33:22** "The host of heaven cannot be numbered and the sands of the sea cannot be measured" is as in Genesis 15:5 and 22:17.
- **33:24** "The two clans" translates *shetei ha-mishpachot*.
- **33:25** "The fixed order" translates *chuqqot*, as in 31:35-36.
- **33:26** "I will restore their fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevutam*. See entry H-SHUV. "Will have mercy" translates *richamtim*.
- **34:1** "All the kingdoms of the earth under his dominion" translates *kol mamlekhot erets memshelet yado*. The chapter is of the siege, about 588 before Christ.
- **34:3** "Eye to eye ... face to face" is as in 32:4.
- **34:5** "In peace" translates *be-shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "As spices were burned for your fathers" refers to the custom of burning spices at the burial of a king (see 2 Chronicles 16:14 and 21:19). "Alas, lord" translates *hoy adon*, as in 22:18. The word is not fulfilled in the death of Zedekiah in Babylon (see 39:7 and 52:11), and the footnote does not decide the matter.
- **34:7** "Lachish and Azekah" are cities of the Judean lowland, west of Jerusalem.
- **34:8** "A covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT. "A proclamation of liberty" translates *liqro lahem deror*. See also Leviticus 25:10 and Isaiah 61:1.
- **34:9** "His Hebrew slaves" translates *avdo ve-shifchato ha-Ivri ve-ha-Ivriyyah*.
- **34:11** "They turned around" translates *vayyashuvu*, and "took back" translates *vayyashivu*, from the same root *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **34:14** "At the end of seven years" translates *mi-qetz sheva shanim*. See also Exodus 21:2 and Deuteronomy 15:12.
- **34:15** "You recently turned" translates *va-tashuvu ata*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **34:16** "You turned around" translates *vattashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV. "Profaned" translates *vattechallelu*.
- **34:17** "Liberty to the sword" translates *deror el-ha-cherev*, in a play on the word of verses 8 and 15. "A horror" translates *za'avah*.
- **34:18** "The calf that they cut in two and passed between its parts" translates *ha-egel asher karetu li-shnayim va-ya'avru bein betarav*. See also Genesis 15:9-10, 17. The phrase "cut a covenant" (*karat berit*) is the Hebrew idiom for making one.
- **34:19** "The eunuchs" translates *ha-sarisim*.
- **34:20** "Those who seek their lives" translates *mevaqshei nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **34:21** "Which has withdrawn from you" translates *ha-olim me-aleikhem*. See also 37:5.
- **34:22** "Will bring them back" translates *vahashivotim*, from *shuv*.
- **35:2** "The Rechabites" translates *ha-Rekhavim*, and "chambers" translates *lishkot*. The chapter is of the reign of Jehoiakim, before 36:1 in the order of events.
- **35:3** "Jaazaniah the son of Jeremiah" is not the prophet.
- **35:4** "The man of God" translates *ish ha-Elohim*, and "keeper of the threshold" translates *shomer ha-saf*.
- **35:6** "Jonadab the son of Rechab" translates *Yehonadav ben-Rekhav*. See also 2 Kings 10:15-23.
- **35:7** "Where you live as foreigners" translates *asher attem gerim sham*.
- **35:11** "The army of Syria" translates *cheil Aram*.
- **35:13** "Receive instruction" translates *tiqchu musar*.
- **35:15** "Turn" translates *shuvu*. See entry H-SHUV. "Amend" translates *heitivu*.
- **35:19** "Will never lack a man to stand before me" translates *lo-yikkaret ish le-Yonadav ben-Rekhav omed lefanai kol-ha-yamim*, and the sense (service, or descent) is not stated.
- **36:1** "The fourth year of Jehoiakim" is 605 before Christ, as in 25:1.
- **36:2** "A scroll" translates *megillat-sefer*.
- **36:3** "Turn" translates *yashuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "I may forgive" translates *ve-salachti*.
- **36:4** "At the dictation of Jeremiah" translates *mi-pi Yirmeyahu*, literally "from the mouth of Jeremiah".
- **36:5** "I am banned" translates *ani atsur*.
- **36:6** "A day of fasting" translates *yom tsom*.
- **36:7** "Their plea for mercy" translates *techinnatam*. "Will turn" translates *yashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **36:9** "The fifth year ... in the ninth month" is about December 604 before Christ.
- **36:10** "The secretary" translates *ha-sofer*, and "the New Gate" is as in 26:10.
- **36:12** "Elnathan the son of Achbor" is as in 26:22.
- **36:14** "Jehudi the son of Nethaniah, son of Shelemiah, son of Cushi" translates *Yehudi ben-Netanyahu ben-Shelemyahu ben-Kushi*.
- **36:22** "The winter house" translates *beit ha-choref*, and "the brazier" translates *ach*.
- **36:23** "Columns" translates *delatot*, literally "doors". "A knife" translates *ta'ar ha-sofer*, "the secretary's knife".
- **36:24** "Nor did they tear their garments" is in contrast with 2 Kings 22:11.
- **36:26** "The king's son" translates *ben-ha-melekh*, which may be a title.
- **36:30** "His dead body will be cast out" is as in 22:19, and the footnote records that 2 Kings 24:6 says that Jehoiakim "slept with his fathers".
- **36:32** "Many similar words were added to them" translates *va-yivvasef aleihem od devarim rabbim kahemmah*.
- **37:1** "Coniah" translates *Konyahu*, as in 22:24. See also 2 Kings 24:17.
- **37:3** "Jehucal the son of Shelemiah" translates *Yehukhal ben-Shelemyahu*, who is called Jucal in 38:1.
- **37:5** "The army of Pharaoh" is that of Pharaoh Hophra (see 44:30). The siege was lifted for a short time in 588 before Christ.
- **37:7** "To inquire of me" translates *lidrosh oti*.
- **37:10** "Wounded men" translates *mudqarim*.
- **37:12** "To receive his portion there among the people" translates *la-challeq mi-sham be-tokh ha-am*, which is hard and may be read "to slip away from there among the people".
- **37:13** "The Benjamin Gate" translates *sha'ar Binyamin*, and "a sentry" translates *ba'al pequddot*. "Irijah" translates *Yir'iyyah*. "Deserting" translates *nofel*, literally "falling".
- **37:15** "They beat him" translates *vayyakku oto*.
- **37:16** "The dungeon cells" translates *ha-bor ve-el ha-chaniyyot*, which is hard.
- **37:17** "Secretly" translates *ba-seter*.
- **37:21** "The street of the bakers" translates *chuts ha-ofim*.
- **38:1** "Gedaliah the son of Pashhur" and "Pashhur the son of Malchiah" are officials and not the Pashhur of 20:1.
- **38:2** "His life as a prize of war" is as in 21:9. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **38:4** "Weakening the hands" translates *mervakh et-yedei*. "Welfare" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **38:6** "The cistern" translates *ha-bor*, "Malchiah, the king's son" translates *Malkiyyahu ben-ha-melekh*, and "mud" translates *tit*.
- **38:7** "Ebed-melech" translates *Eved-Melekh*, "the Cushite" translates *ha-Kushi*, and "a eunuch" translates *saris*.
- **38:11** "A wardrobe" translates *tachat ha-otsar*, and "old rags and worn-out clothes" translates *belo'ei sechavot u-belo'ei maltachim*. The words are rare.
- **38:12** "Armpits" translates *atsilot yadekha*.
- **38:14** "The third entrance" translates *mavo ha-shelishi*, which is not known.
- **38:16** "Who made our souls" translates *asher asah-lanu et-ha-nefesh ha-zot*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **38:17** "Surrender" translates *yatsa el*, literally "go out to".
- **38:19** "Deal cruelly with me" translates *hit'allelu bi*.
- **38:22** "Your trusted friends" translates *anshei shelomekha*, literally "the men of your peace", and "have deceived you" translates *hissituka*.
- **39:1** "The ninth year of Zedekiah ... in the tenth month" is January 588 before Christ.
- **39:2** "The eleventh year ... in the fourth month, on the ninth day" is July 586 before Christ. "A breach was made" translates *hubqe'ah*.
- **39:3** "The Middle Gate" translates *sha'ar ha-tavekh*. The names and titles of the officials (*Nergal-sar-ezer*, *Samgar-nebo*, *Sarsechim* and the titles *Rab-saris* and *Rab-mag*) are uncertain, and the Hebrew text of the verse is hard.
- **39:4** "The Arabah" is the rift valley that goes down to the Jordan.
- **39:5** "Riblah" translates *Rivlah*, a place in the land of Hamath. "He passed sentence on him" translates *va-yedabber ito mishpatim*.
- **39:6** "Killed the sons of Zedekiah ... before his eyes" is rendered as written.
