- **39:7** "He put out the eyes of Zedekiah" is rendered as written. See also 52:11 and 2 Kings 25:7.
- **39:8** "The house of the people" translates *beit ha-am*.
- **39:9** "Nebuzaradan" translates *Nevuzar'adan*, and "the captain of the guard" translates *rav tabbachim*, literally "chief of the cooks" or "of the executioners".
- **39:10** "The poor people" translates *ha-am ha-dallim*.
- **39:12** "Look after him well" translates *qachenu ve-einekha sim alav*.
- **39:13** The verses 4 to 13 are not in the Greek.
- **39:14** "Gedaliah the son of Ahikam" is as in 26:24 and 40:5.
- **39:16** "For harm and not for good" translates *le-ra'ah ve-lo le-tovah*.
- **39:18** "Your life as a prize of war" translates *nafshekha le-shalal*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **40:1** "Ramah" translates *Ramah*, a place north of Jerusalem (see 31:15). The account of the release of Jeremiah here differs from 39:11-14, where he is handed to Gedaliah in Jerusalem, and the footnote does not decide how the two are joined. "In chains" translates *ba-aziqqim*.
- **40:2** "The captain of the guard" translates *rav tabbachim*, as in 39:9.
- **40:4** "I will look after you well" translates *ve-asim einai alekha*.
- **40:5** "Governor" translates *va-yafqed*, literally "appointed". "An allowance of food" translates *arukhah*, and "a present" translates *mas'et*.
- **40:6** "Mizpah" translates *ha-Mitspah*, a town of Benjamin, north of Jerusalem.
- **40:7** "The captains of the forces in the open country" translates *sarei ha-chayalim asher ba-sadeh*.
- **40:9** "Serve" translates *avdu*.
- **40:10** "To represent you" translates *la-amod lifnei*, and "summer fruits" translates *qayits*.
- **40:12** "Returned" translates *vayyashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **40:14** "Baalis the king of the Ammonites" is named only here. "To take your life" translates *le-hakkotkha nafesh*, literally "to strike you in life". See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **40:15** "Secretly" translates *ba-seter*.
- **41:1** "In the seventh month" is in 586 or a few years later, and the year is not stated. "Of the royal family" translates *mi-zera ha-melukhah*.
- **41:5** "Their beards shaved and their clothes torn, and with gashes cut on themselves" translates *megulle'i zaqan ve-qeru'ei vegadim u-mitgoddedim*, signs of mourning, and the men may have come to mourn the temple. "Grain offerings and incense" translates *minchah u-levonah*.
- **41:7** "A cistern" translates *bor*. It is rendered as written.
- **41:9** "King Asa" built the pool against Baasha (see 1 Kings 15:16-22).
- **41:10** "The princesses" translates *benot ha-melekh*.
- **41:12** "The great pool that is in Gibeon" translates *mayim rabbim asher be-Giv'on*.
- **41:14** "Turned around" translates *vayyasobbu vayyashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **41:17** "Geruth Chimham" translates *Gerut Kimham*, which is hard and may be read "the lodging place of Chimham".
- **42:1** "Jezaniah the son of Hoshaiah" is called Azariah the son of Hoshaiah in 43:2, and the Greek has "Azariah" in both.
- **42:2** "Our plea for mercy" translates *tehinnatenu*.
- **42:5** "A true and faithful witness" translates *ed emet ve-ne'eman*.
- **42:7** "At the end of ten days" translates *mi-qetz yamim asarah*.
- **42:10** "I will build you up and not pull you down. I will plant you, and not pluck you up" uses the verbs of 1:10. "I relent" translates *nichamti*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **42:12** "I will grant you mercy" translates *ve-natati lakhem rachamim*, and "bring you back" translates *ve-heshiv etkhem*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **42:18** "An execration, a horror, a curse and a taunt" translates *le-alah u-le-shammah u-le-qelalah u-le-cherpah*.
- **42:20** "You have gone astray at the cost of your lives" translates *hit'item be-nafshotekhem*, which is hard. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **43:2** "Azariah the son of Hoshaiah" is called Jezaniah the son of Hoshaiah in 42:1. "The insolent people" translates *kol-ha-anashim ha-zedim*.
- **43:3** "Is inciting you" translates *massit otkha*.
- **43:5** "Who had returned" translates *shavu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **43:7** "Tahpanhes" translates *Tachpanches*, a border city of Egypt in the delta (see also 2:16).
- **43:9** "The mortar in the pavement" translates *ba-melet ba-malben*, which is hard.
- **43:10** "My servant" translates *avdi*, as in 25:9 and 27:6. "His royal canopy" translates *shaphrir*, a rare word.
- **43:11** "Giving over to death those who are for death, to captivity those who are for captivity, and to the sword those who are for the sword" is as in 15:2.
- **43:12** "Temples" translates *batei*, "houses". "He will clean the land of Egypt as a shepherd cleans his cloak of vermin" translates *ve-atah et-erets Mitsrayim ka-asher ye'teh ha-ro'eh et-bigdo*, in which the verb *atah* may mean "wrap" or "delouse", and the line is hard. "In peace" translates *be-shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **43:13** "The obelisks of Heliopolis" translates *matstsevot Beit-Shemesh*, "the pillars of the house of the sun", a city of Egypt (On).
- **44:1** "Migdol" and "Tahpanhes" are cities of the delta, "Memphis" translates *Nof*, and "Pathros" is Upper Egypt.
- **44:4** "Persistently" translates *hashkem*.
- **44:5** "To turn" translates *lashuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **44:7** "Infant" translates *olel*.
- **44:10** "They have not humbled themselves" translates *lo dukku*. "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH. "My statutes" translates *chuqqotai*.
- **44:12** "An execration, a horror, a curse, and a taunt" is as in 42:18.
- **44:14** "Return" translates *lashuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "Fugitives" translates *peletim*.
- **44:17** "The queen of heaven" translates *melekhet ha-shamayim*, as in 7:18. "We had plenty of food, and prospered" translates *va-nisba lechem va-nihyeh tovim*.
- **44:19** "Cakes for her bearing her image" translates *kavvanim le-ha'atsivah*, in which the verb is rare and may mean "to shape her". "Without our husbands' approval" translates *be-li anshenu*.
- **44:23** "His teaching ... his statutes ... his testimonies" translates *be-torato u-vechuqqotav u-ve-edvotav*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **44:26** "My great name" translates *shemi ha-gadol*. "As the Lord GOD lives" translates *chai Adonai YHWH*.
- **44:27** "I am watching over them" translates *shoqed ani aleihem*, as in 1:12.
- **44:28** "Will return" translates *yashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV. "Few in number" translates *metei mispar*.
- **44:30** "Pharaoh Hophra" translates *Par'oh Chofra*, who reigned from 589 to 570 before Christ. "His life" translates *nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **45:1** "The fourth year of Jehoiakim" is 605 before Christ, as in 36:1. In the Greek this chapter stands after the oracles against the nations.
- **45:3** "Sorrow" translates *yagon*, "my pain" translates *makh'ovi*, and "my groaning" translates *anchati*.
- **45:4** "What I have built I am breaking down, and what I have planted I am plucking up" uses the verbs of 1:10.
- **45:5** "Great things" translates *gedolot*. "Your life as a prize of war" is as in 21:9, 38:2 and 39:18. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **46:1** "Concerning the nations" translates *al-ha-goyim*. In the Greek the oracles stand after 25:13, in a different order.
- **46:2** "Pharaoh Neco" translates *Par'oh Nekho*, who reigned from 610 to 595 before Christ. "The fourth year of Jehoiakim" is 605 before Christ, the year of the battle of Carchemish (see also 25:1).
- **46:3** "Buckler and shield" translates *magen ve-tsinnah*.
- **46:5** "Terror on every side" translates *magor mi-saviv*, as in 6:25, 20:3, 20:10 and 49:29.
- **46:7** "The Nile" translates *ke-ye'or*.
- **46:9** "Cush", "Put" and "Lud" are peoples allied to Egypt (see also Isaiah 66:19 and Ezekiel 30:5).
- **46:10** "A day of vengeance" translates *yom neqamah*, and "holds a sacrifice" translates *zevach*. See also Isaiah 34:6 and Zephaniah 1:7.
- **46:11** "Balm" translates *tsori*, as in 8:22. "Young daughter of Egypt" translates *betulat bat-Mitsrayim*. The rendering of *betulah* is open to the team's decision.
- **46:14** "Migdol", "Memphis" (*Nof*) and "Tahpanhes" are as in 44:1.
- **46:15** "Why has Apis fled? Why did your bull not stand?" follows the Greek, and *Apis* is the sacred bull of Memphis. The Hebrew text has *maddua nisechaph abbirekha lo amad*, "why has your strong one been swept away? He did not stand". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **46:16** "The sword of the oppressor" translates *cherev ha-yonah*, as in 25:38 and 50:16.
- **46:17** "Noisy one who lets the hour go by" translates *sha'on he'evir ha-mo'ed*, which is a play on the name of Pharaoh Hophra and is hard.
- **46:18** "The King" translates *ha-Melekh*.
- **46:20** "A biting fly" translates *qerets*, which is hard.
- **46:21** "Her hired soldiers" translates *sekhireha*.
- **46:25** "Amon of Thebes" translates *Amon mi-No*, the god of the city of Thebes (see also Nahum 3:8).
- **46:26** "Afterward Egypt will be inhabited as in the days of old" translates *ve-acharei khen tishkon kime-qedem*. See also Ezekiel 29:13-14.
- **46:27** The verses 27 and 28 are as in 30:10-11, and the wording is matched. "Will return" translates *shav*, and in both places it is not tagged.
- **47:1** "Before Pharaoh struck down Gaza" refers to a campaign that is not named, and the date is uncertain.
- **47:2** "Waters are rising out of the north" is a figure of an army.
- **47:4** "Caphtor" translates *Kaftor*, which is Crete or a land of the Aegean.
- **47:5** "Baldness" translates *qorchah*, a sign of mourning (see also 16:6 and 48:37). "Remnant of their valley" translates *she'erit imqam*, which is hard, and the Greek has "Anakim". "Gash yourselves" translates *titgoddedi*.
- **47:6** "The sword of the LORD" translates *cherev YHWH*.
- **48:1** "Nebo" and "Kiriathaim" are cities of Moab. "The fortress" translates *ha-misgav*.
- **48:2** "In Heshbon they planned disaster" translates *be-Cheshbon chashevu ra'ah*, in a play on the name of the city. "Madmen" translates *Madmen*, a place of Moab, and the word is also a play (*tiddommi*, "you will be silent").
- **48:5** "Luhith" and "Horonaim" are places of Moab (see also Isaiah 15:5).
- **48:6** "A juniper in the desert" translates *ke-ar'ar ba-midbar*, which is hard and may be read "like a wild donkey".
- **48:7** "Chemosh" translates *Kemosh*, the god of Moab.
- **48:10** "Cursed is the one who is slack in doing the work of the LORD" is rendered as written.
- **48:11** "His dregs" translates *shemarav*.
- **48:13** "Bethel, their confidence" refers to the calf at Bethel (see 1 Kings 12:29).
- **48:17** "Sceptre" translates *matteh*, and "staff" translates *maqqel*.
- **48:18** "Dibon" is as in Isaiah 15:9 in the draft, where the Hebrew text has "Dimon".
- **48:21** The places in verses 21 to 24 (*Holon*, *Jahzah*, *Mephaath*, *Beth-diblathaim*, *Beth-gamul*, *Beth-meon*, *Kerioth*, *Bozrah*) are cities of the tableland of Moab, and some are not known.
- **48:26** "Make him drunk" translates *hashkiruhu*, and "will wallow" translates *ve-safaq*.
- **48:28** "The dove" translates *yonah*.
- **48:29** The verses 29 to 33 are close to Isaiah 16:6-10, and the wording is open to be matched with Isaiah 15 and 16.
- **48:31** "Kir-heres" translates *Qir-Cheres*, a city of Moab.
- **48:32** "Jazer" and "Sibmah" are places of Moab (see also Isaiah 16:8-9).
- **48:33** "The winepresses" translates *yeqavim*.
- **48:34** "Eglath-shelishiyah" translates *Eglat shelishiyyah*, which may be a place or "the third heifer".
- **48:35** "The high place" translates *bamah*.
- **48:36** "A flute" translates *chalil*.
- **48:37** The shaven head, the cut beard, the gashes and the sackcloth are as in 47:5 and 16:6.
- **48:38** "A vessel for which no one cares" is as in 22:28.
- **48:40** The verses 40 and 41 are as in 49:22.
- **48:43** "Terror, pit and trap" translates *pachad va-fachat va-fach*, as in Isaiah 24:17-18.
- **48:45** The verses 45 and 46 are close to Numbers 21:28-29 and 24:17. "The forehead of Moab" translates *pe'at Mo'av*, "the crown of the sons of tumult" translates *qodqod benei sha'on*, and "Sihon" is the king of Heshbon.
- **48:47** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ve-shavti shevut*. See entry H-SHUV. "Thus far is the judgement on Moab" translates *ad-henna mishpat Mo'av*.
- **49:1** "Milcom" translates *Milkom*, the god of the Ammonites (see 1 Kings 11:5 and 11:33). The Hebrew vowel pointing has *malkam*, "their king", which has the same letters, and the Greek has "Milcom". The WABV text reads the name in 49:1 and 49:3, and it is flagged for the scholar as a reading of the vowels and not of the letters. "Gad" is the tribe whose land the Ammonites took after 733 before Christ.
- **49:2** "Rabbah" translates *Rabbat bene-Ammon*, the capital of Ammon, and "the battle cry" translates *teru'at milchamah*. "Will dispossess" translates *ve-yarash*.
- **49:3** "Ai" translates *ha-Ai*, which is not the Ai of Joshua and is not known.
- **49:4** "Faithless daughter" translates *ha-bat ha-shovevah*, from *shuv*, as in 31:22.
- **49:6** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **49:7** "Teman" translates *Teman*, a place in Edom. The verses 7 to 22 are close to Obadiah 1-9, and the wording is open to be matched when Obadiah is drafted.
- **49:8** "Dedan" is a people of the Arabian desert.
- **49:9** "Gleanings" translates *olelot*, as in Obadiah 5.
- **49:12** "The cup" is as in 25:15-29.
- **49:13** "I have sworn by myself" translates *bi nishba'ti*, as in 22:5. "Bozrah" is the chief city of Edom.
- **49:14** The verses 14 to 16 are as in Obadiah 1-4, and the wording is open to be matched.
- **49:16** "The horror you inspire" translates *tifletsetkha*, a rare word.
- **49:17** "A horror ... will hiss" translates *le-shammah ... yishrok*, as in 19:8 and 50:13.
- **49:18** "As when Sodom and Gomorrah and their neighbour cities were overthrown" translates *ke-mahpekhat Sedom ve-Amorah ve-shekhenehah*. In 50:40 the Hebrew has "as when God overthrew" (*ke-mahpekhat Elohim*), and the draft keeps the difference.
- **49:19** The verses 19 to 21 are as in 50:44-46, and the wording is matched, except that the Hebrew has "him" here and "them" in 50:44.
- **49:20** "The plan" translates *etsat*, and "the purposes" translates *machshevot*.
- **49:21** "The Red Sea" translates *Yam Suf*.
- **49:22** The verse is as in 48:40-41.
- **49:23** "Damascus", "Hamath" and "Arpad" are cities of Aram (Syria).
- **49:25** "The famous city" translates *ir tehillah*, which is hard.
- **49:27** "Ben-hadad" translates *Ben-Hadad*, a name of the kings of Aram.
- **49:28** "Kedar" translates *Qedar*, a people of the Arabian desert. "Hazor" translates *Chatsor*, which is not the Hazor of Galilee. "The people of the east" translates *benei-qedem*.
- **49:29** "Terror on every side" translates *magor mi-saviv*, as in 6:25 and 46:5.
- **49:32** "Those who cut the corners of their hair" translates *qetsutsei pe'ah*, as in 9:26 and 25:23.
- **49:33** "A haunt of jackals" translates *me'on tannim*, as in 9:11 and 10:22.
- **49:34** "The beginning of the reign of Zedekiah" is 597 before Christ.
- **49:36** "The four winds" translates *arba ruchot*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **49:37** "Those who seek their life" translates *mevaqshei nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **49:39** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **50:1** "Babylon" translates *Bavel*, and "the Chaldeans" translates *Kasdim*.
- **50:2** "Bel" and "Merodach" translate *Bel* and *Merodakh*, the chief gods of Babylon. "Her idols" translates *gillulehā*, a word that means "dung gods".
- **50:4** "Weeping as they come" translates *halokh u-vakho yelekhu*.
- **50:5** "An everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **50:6** "Lost sheep" translates *tson ovedot*.
- **50:7** "Their habitation of righteousness" translates *neveh-tsedeq*, as in 31:23. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **50:8** "Male goats" translates *attudim*.
- **50:9** "A skilled warrior who does not return empty-handed" translates *ke-gibbor maskil lo yashuv reqam*, which is hard, and the Greek differs.
- **50:15** "She has surrendered" translates *natenah yado*, literally "she has given her hand". "Do to her as she has done" is the law of like for like.
- **50:16** "The sword of the oppressor" translates *cherev ha-yonah*, as in 25:38 and 46:16.
- **50:17** "Has gnawed his bones" translates *itstsemo*.
- **50:19** "I will restore" translates *ve-hashivoti*, from *shuv*. "His desire" translates *nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **50:20** "I will pardon" translates *eslach*.
- **50:21** "Merathaim" and "Pekod" are names that also mean "double rebellion" and "punishment", and they are in a play on the words. "Devote them to destruction" translates *hacharem*.
- **50:23** "The hammer of the whole earth" translates *pattish kol-ha-arets*.
- **50:24** "A snare" translates *yoqesh*.
- **50:25** "His armoury" translates *otsaro*.
- **50:26** "Her granaries" translates *mavvu'eha*, which is hard.
- **50:27** "Her bulls" translates *parehā*.
- **50:28** "Vengeance for his temple" is as in 51:11.
- **50:29** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *Qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **50:30** The verse is as in 49:26.
- **50:31** "Proud one" translates *zadon*.
- **50:34** "Their Redeemer" translates *go'alam*. See entry H-GOEL. "He will surely plead their cause" translates *riv yariv et-rivam*, and "give rest" translates *hirgia*.
- **50:35** "Her wise" translates *chakhameha*.
- **50:36** "The diviners" translates *ha-baddim*, which is hard.
- **50:38** "A drought against her waters" follows the Greek and the vowels *choreb*. The Hebrew vowel pointing has *cherev*, "a sword", which has the same letters. The WABV text reads the vowels of the Greek and is flagged for the scholar as a reading of the vowels. "Images" translates *pesilim*.
- **50:39** "Wild beasts" translates *tsiyyim*, "hyenas" translates *iyyim*, and "ostriches" translates *benot ya'anah*. See also Isaiah 13:21-22.
- **50:40** "As when God overthrew Sodom and Gomorrah" translates *ke-mahpekhat Elohim et-Sedom ve-et-Amorah*. See the footnote at 49:18.
- **50:41** The verses 41 to 43 are as in 6:22-24, and the wording is matched.
- **50:44** The verses 44 to 46 are as in 49:19-21, and the wording is matched.
- **51:1** "The spirit of a destroyer" translates *ruach mashchit*. See entry H-RUACH. "Leb-qamai" translates *Lev Qamai*, which is a coded name for the Chaldeans (*Kasdim*), in which the letters of the name are replaced by those in the opposite place in the alphabet, as in *Sheshach* (see 25:26).
- **51:2** "Winnowers" is read for the Hebrew vowel pointing *zarim*, "strangers", which has the same letters as *zorim*, "winnowers", and the verb "winnow" (*ve-zeruha*) follows. The WABV text is flagged for the scholar as a reading of the vowels.
- **51:3** "Let not the archer bend his bow" translates *el-yidrokh ha-doreikh qashto*, which is hard.
- **51:5** "Have not been forsaken" translates *lo-alman*, and "the Holy One of Israel" translates *Qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **51:6** "Save his life" translates *malletu ish nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Vengeance" translates *neqamah*, and "repayment" translates *gemul*.
- **51:7** "A golden cup" is as in 25:15, with the figure of the nations that drank.
- **51:8** "Balm" translates *tsori*, as in 8:22 and 46:11.
- **51:10** "Our vindication" translates *tsidqotenu*, literally "our righteous acts". See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **51:11** "The spirit of the kings of the Medes" translates *ruach malkhei Madai*. See entry H-RUACH. "Vengeance for his temple" translates *neqamat heikhalo*, as in 50:28.
- **51:13** "Who live by many waters" translates *shokhenet al-mayim rabbim*, and "the thread of your life is cut" translates *ammat betsa'ekh*, which is hard.
- **51:15** The verses 15 to 19 are as in 10:12-16, and the wording is matched.
- **51:20** "My hammer and weapon of war" translates *matstsev attah li kelei milchamah*. The one who is addressed is not named in the Hebrew. See also 50:23.
- **51:25** "Destroying mountain" translates *har ha-mashchit*, and "a burnt mountain" translates *har serefah*.
- **51:27** "Ararat", "Minni" and "Ashkenaz" are kingdoms of the north of Mesopotamia. "A marshal" translates *tiphsar*, and "bristling locusts" translates *yeleq samar*.
- **51:28** "The kings of the Medes" translates *malkhei Madai*.
- **51:31** "From end to end" translates *miqtseh*.
- **51:32** "The marshes" translates *agamim*.
- **51:33** "A threshing floor" translates *goren*.
- **51:34** "A monster" translates *tannin*.
- **51:35** "The violence done to me" translates *chamasi*, and "my kinsfolk" translates *she'eri*, literally "my flesh".
- **51:36** "I will plead your cause" translates *hineni ar riveikh*.
- **51:37** "A heap of ruins" translates *gallim*, and "a haunt of jackals" translates *me'on tannim*, as in 9:11 and 49:33.
- **51:41** "Sheshach" is as in 25:26, and it is a coded name for Babylon.
- **51:44** "Bel" translates *Bel*, as in 50:2.
- **51:45** "Save his life" translates *malletu ish nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **51:46** "The report" translates *ha-shemu'ah*.
- **51:51** "The holy places" translates *miqdeshei*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **51:56** "A God of recompense" translates *El gemulot*.
- **51:58** "The broad wall of Babylon" translates *chomot Bavel ha-rechavah*. "The peoples labour for nothing" is as in Habakkuk 2:13.
- **51:59** "The fourth year of his reign" of Zedekiah is 593 before Christ. "Seraiah the son of Neriah" is the brother of Baruch. "The quartermaster" translates *sar menuchah*, which is hard.
- **51:60** "Jeremiah wrote in a book" translates *va-yiktov Yirmeyahu et-kol-ha-ra'ah ... el-sefer echad*.
- **51:63** "Tie a stone to it" translates *ve-qashartah alav even*, and "the Euphrates" translates *Perat*.
- **51:64** "Will sink" translates *tishqa*, and "they will weary themselves" translates *ve-ya'afu*, which is not in the Greek. "Thus far are the words of Jeremiah" translates *ad-henna divrei Yirmeyahu*, and chapter 52 is an addition that follows.
- **52:1** The verses 1 to 3 are as in 2 Kings 24:18-20, and the wording is open to be matched with the WABV 2 Kings. "Hamutal the daughter of Jeremiah of Libnah" translates *Chamutal bat-Yirmeyahu mi-Livnah*, who was also the mother of Jehoahaz (see 2 Kings 23:31).
- **52:2** "According to all that Jehoiakim had done" translates *ke-khol asher-asah Yehoyaqim*.
- **52:3** "Things came to the point" translates *hayetah*, and "he cast them out from his presence" translates *hishlikho otam me-al panav*. The verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **52:4** The verses 4 to 16 are as in 2 Kings 25:1-12, and they are close to 39:1-10. "The ninth year ... in the tenth month, on the tenth day" is January 588 before Christ. "Siegeworks" translates *dayeq*.
- **52:6** "On the ninth day of the fourth month" is July 586 before Christ, and the verse is as in 39:2 and 2 Kings 25:3.
- **52:7** "A breach was made" translates *va-tibbaqa*, and "the gate between the two walls" translates *sha'ar bein ha-chomotayim*. See also 39:4. "The Arabah" is the rift valley that goes down to the Jordan.
- **52:9** "Riblah" translates *Rivlah*, as in 39:5. "He passed sentence on him" translates *va-yedabber ito mishpatim*.
- **52:10** "Killed the sons of Zedekiah before his eyes" is rendered as written, as in 39:6.
- **52:11** "He put out the eyes of Zedekiah" is rendered as written, as in 39:7. "In chains" translates *ba-nechushtayim*, and "prison" translates *beit ha-pequddot*. The words "till the day of his death" are not in 39:7 or in 2 Kings 25:7.
- **52:12** "In the fifth month, on the tenth day" is August 586 before Christ, and 2 Kings 25:8 has "on the seventh day". "The nineteenth year of King Nebuchadnezzar" is 587 or 586 before Christ. "The captain of the guard" translates *rav tabbachim*, as in 39:9.
- **52:13** "Every great house" translates *kol-beit gadol*, and the burning of the house of the LORD is rendered as written.
- **52:15** "Some of the poorest of the people" translates *middallot ha-am*, and "the rest of the artisans" translates *yeter ha-amon*, in which the word *amon* is hard, and 2 Kings 25:11 has *ha-hamon*, "the multitude".
- **52:16** "Vinedressers and ploughmen" translates *kormim u-yogevim*, as in 2 Kings 25:12.
- **52:17** "The pillars" translates *ammudei*, "the stands" translates *ha-mekhonot*, and "the bronze sea" translates *yam ha-nechoshet*. The verses 17 to 23 are as in 2 Kings 25:13-17, and see also 27:19 and 1 Kings 7:15-50.
- **52:18** The names of the vessels (*ha-sirot ve-ha-yaim ve-ha-mezammerot ve-ha-mizraqot ve-ha-kappot*) are rare, and the Hebrew words are not all known.
- **52:20** "Twelve bronze bulls" translates *shenem-asar baqar nechoshet*, and "beyond weight" translates *lo-hayah mishqal*.
- **52:21** "Eighteen cubits" and "twelve cubits" are as in 1 Kings 7:15, and "four fingers thick" translates *ve-avyo arba etsba'ot*.
- **52:22** "A capital of bronze, five cubits" is as in 1 Kings 7:16. 2 Kings 25:17 has "three cubits". "Network" translates *sevakhah*, and "pomegranates" translates *rimmonim*.
- **52:23** "On the sides" translates *ruchah*, literally "toward the wind", and the word is not tagged.
- **52:24** "Seraiah the chief priest" translates *Serayah kohen ha-rosh*, and "Zephaniah the second priest" translates *Tsefanyah kohen ha-mishneh*. See also 21:1, 29:25 and 37:3. The verses 24 to 27 are as in 2 Kings 25:18-21.
- **52:25** "An officer" translates *saris*, and "seven of the king's counsellors" translates *shiv'ah anashim me-ro'ei penei ha-melekh*. 2 Kings 25:19 has "five". "The secretary of the commander of the army" translates *sofer sar ha-tsava*.
- **52:27** "Judah was taken into exile out of its land" translates *va-yigel Yehudah me-al admato*.
- **52:28** The verses 28 to 30 are not in 2 Kings and not in the Greek. "The seventh year" of Nebuchadnezzar is 598 or 597 before Christ, "the eighteenth year" is 587 or 586, and "the twenty-third year" is 582 or 581. The numbers do not agree with 2 Kings 24:14-16, and the footnote does not decide how they are joined.
- **52:31** "The thirty-seventh year of the exile of Jehoiachin" is 561 before Christ. "Evil-merodach" translates *Evil-Merodakh*, who reigned from 562 to 560 before Christ. "Graciously freed" translates *nasa et-rosh*, literally "lifted up the head". The verses 31 to 34 are as in 2 Kings 25:27-30.
- **52:32** "He spoke kindly to him" translates *va-yedabber ito tovot*.
- **52:33** "Prison garments" translates *bigdei kelo'o*.
- **52:34** "A regular allowance" translates *arukhat tamid*, and "as long as he lived" translates *kol yemei chayyav*.

| 5:1-31 | Genesis 18:22-33; Ezekiel 22:30; Psalm 14:1-3; Amos 3:7; Micah 7:2; Isaiah 5:8-10; Jeremiah 14:13-16 | Parallel and allusion: the search for one person, the false prophets, and the nation from afar |
| 6:1-30 | Jeremiah 8:11; Ezekiel 13:10; Isaiah 1:11-15; Amos 5:21-24; Isaiah 1:21-26; Ezekiel 22:17-22; Matthew 11:28-29 | Parallel and allusion: the siege, "peace, peace", the ancient paths, and the refining that fails |
| 7:1-15 | Jeremiah 26:1-19; Psalm 78:60; 1 Samuel 4:1-11; Matthew 21:12-13; Mark 11:15-17; Luke 19:45-46; Exodus 20:13-16; Hosea 4:2; Micah 3:12 | Parallel and allusion: the temple sermon, the den of robbers, and Shiloh |
| 7:16-34 | Jeremiah 11:14; 14:11; 15:1; 44:15-25; Amos 5:25; Hosea 6:6; Exodus 6:7; Leviticus 26:12; 18:21; 2 Kings 23:10; Jeremiah 19:1-13; 16:9; 25:10; 33:11 | Parallel and allusion: no intercession, the queen of heaven, the covenant formula, and Topheth |
| 8:1-3 | 2 Kings 23:16; Jeremiah 16:4; 22:19; Deuteronomy 4:19 | Parallel and allusion: the bones brought out of the tombs |
| 8:4-13 | Jeremiah 3:1-14; 5:3; Isaiah 1:3; Jeremiah 6:13-15; Isaiah 5:24; Jeremiah 14:13-16 | Parallel: return and turning away, the stork that knows her times, and "peace, peace" |
| 8:14-22 | Jeremiah 9:15; 4:19-21; 14:19; 46:11; Genesis 37:25; 1 Kings 14:22-24 | Parallel and allusion: the poisoned water, the lament, and the balm in Gilead |
| 9:1-11 | Jeremiah 14:17-18; Lamentations 2:11, 18; Psalm 55:6-8; Jeremiah 5:1-6; Micah 7:5-6; Jeremiah 4:7; 10:22 | Parallel and allusion: the weeping prophet, the treacherous neighbours, and the lair of jackals |
| 9:12-26 | Hosea 14:9; Deuteronomy 29:24-28; Jeremiah 16:10-13; 1 Corinthians 1:31; 2 Corinthians 10:17; Isaiah 5:15-16; Jeremiah 4:4; Deuteronomy 10:16; Leviticus 19:27 | Parallel and allusion: the mourning women, "let the one who boasts boast in this", and the circumcision of the heart |
| 10:1-16 | Isaiah 40:18-20; 44:9-20; 46:6-7; Psalm 115:3-8; 135:15-18; Habakkuk 2:18-19; Jeremiah 51:15-19; Psalm 135:7; Deuteronomy 32:9 | Parallel: the folly of idols, the true God, and the maker of the earth |
| 10:17-25 | Jeremiah 6:1; 4:19-21; 6:22; Psalm 79:6-7; Proverbs 16:9; Psalm 6:1; Jeremiah 30:11; 46:28 | Parallel and allusion: the bundle under siege, the way of human beings, and the prayer against the nations |
| 11:1-17 | Deuteronomy 27:26; 28:15-68; 4:20; Exodus 19:5-6; Jeremiah 7:23; 31:31-32; Hosea 8:1; Jeremiah 7:16; 14:11 | Parallel and allusion: the broken covenant, the curse, and no intercession |
| 11:18-23 | Isaiah 53:7; Jeremiah 12:1-6; 17:10, 18; 20:12; Psalm 7:9; 139:23; Jeremiah 1:1; Isaiah 53:8 | Parallel and allusion: the first confession, the gentle lamb, and the people of Anathoth |
| 12:1-17 | Job 21:7-16; Psalm 73:1-14; Habakkuk 1:13; Jeremiah 5:28; Psalm 37:1-2; Isaiah 5:1-7; Jeremiah 18:7-10; 1:10 | Parallel and allusion: why the wicked prosper, the beloved given to enemies, and the neighbours plucked up |
| 13:1-14 | Isaiah 20:2-4; Jeremiah 19:1-13; Ezekiel 4:1-17; Hosea 1:2-9; Jeremiah 25:15-29; Isaiah 51:17-23 | Parallel and allusion: the linen waistcloth and the jar of wine |
| 13:15-27 | Jeremiah 22:20-30; 2 Kings 24:8-17; Isaiah 47:1-3; Ezekiel 16:37; Hosea 2:10; Nahum 3:5; Jeremiah 2:23-25 | Parallel and allusion: the king and the queen mother, the Cushite's skin, and the shame |
| 14:1-12 | Joel 1:1-20; Psalm 79:9; Jeremiah 3:22; Exodus 32:11-14; Psalm 106:23; Jeremiah 7:16; 11:14 | Parallel and allusion: the drought, the people's prayer, and the refusal |
| 14:13-22 | Jeremiah 5:12-13; 23:9-40; 27:9-18; Deuteronomy 18:20-22; Jeremiah 8:15; 31:31-34; Lamentations 2:11-14 | Parallel and allusion: the false prophets, the lament, and the plea for the covenant |
| 15:1-9 | Exodus 32:11-14; 1 Samuel 7:9; 12:23; Psalm 99:6; 2 Kings 21:1-16; 23:26-27; Ezekiel 14:21; Zechariah 11:9 | Parallel and allusion: Moses and Samuel, the four destroyers, and Manasseh |
| 15:10-21 | Jeremiah 1:18-19; 20:7-18; Job 3:1-10; Psalm 119:103; Ezekiel 3:1-3; Revelation 10:9-10; Psalm 69:7-9 | Parallel and allusion: the confession, the words that were eaten, and the wall of bronze |
| 16:1-13 | Ezekiel 24:15-27; Jeremiah 7:34; 25:10; Leviticus 19:28; Deuteronomy 14:1; Jeremiah 9:12-16; 22:8-9 | Parallel and allusion: no wife or mourning, and the reasons for the exile |
| 16:14-21 | Jeremiah 23:7-8; 3:18; 12:15; Amos 4:2; Habakkuk 1:14-15; Isaiah 2:20; Psalm 18:1-2 | Parallel and allusion: the new exodus, the fishers and hunters, and the nations that will come |
| 17:1-4 | Deuteronomy 9:10; 2 Corinthians 3:3; Jeremiah 31:33; Job 19:24; Jeremiah 15:13-14; Deuteronomy 28:36; Leviticus 26:30 | Parallel and allusion: the sin written with a pen of iron, and the loss of the heritage |
| 17:5-13 | Psalm 1:1-6; 118:8-9; Isaiah 31:1-3; Proverbs 14:12; Jeremiah 11:20; 1 Samuel 16:7; Revelation 2:23; Jeremiah 2:13; 14:8 | Parallel and allusion: the two kinds of person, the deceitful heart, and the fountain of living water |
| 17:14-18 | Psalm 6:2; 41:4; Jeremiah 15:15-18; 11:18-23; 18:19-23; Psalm 35:4-8; 109:6-20 | Parallel and allusion: the prayer for healing, the taunt, and the prayer against the persecutors |
| 17:19-27 | Exodus 20:8-11; Deuteronomy 5:12-15; Nehemiah 13:15-22; Isaiah 56:2; 58:13-14; Amos 8:5; Jeremiah 21:12 | Parallel and allusion: the Sabbath and the gates of Jerusalem |
| 18:1-12 | Isaiah 29:16; 45:9; 64:8; Romans 9:20-21; Jeremiah 1:10; 12:14-17; Jonah 3:10; Ezekiel 18:21-23; Jeremiah 7:3-7 | Parallel and allusion: the potter and the clay, and the nation that turns |
| 18:13-23 | Jeremiah 2:10-13; 19:8; 11:18-23; 20:10; Psalm 35:7; 109:1-5; 140:1-11; Jeremiah 7:16; 15:1 | Parallel and allusion: the horror, the plot against the prophet, and the prayer against his enemies |
| 19:1-15 | Jeremiah 7:30-34; 2 Kings 21:12; 23:10; Deuteronomy 28:53; Lamentations 4:10; Isaiah 30:14; Jeremiah 25:15-29 | Parallel and allusion: the broken flask, Topheth, and the disaster on the city |
| 20:1-6 | Jeremiah 19:14-15; 37:11-16; 38:1-13; Amos 7:10-17; Jeremiah 6:25; 20:10; 46:5; 49:29 | Parallel and allusion: Pashhur, the stocks, and Terror on Every Side |
| 20:7-13 | Jeremiah 15:10-18; 1:6-10, 18-19; 11:18-20; Psalm 22:6-8; Job 16:9-17; Psalm 31:13; Jeremiah 6:25 | Parallel and allusion: the last confession, the fire in the bones, and the praise |
| 20:14-18 | Job 3:1-26; Jeremiah 15:10; Genesis 19:24-25; Ecclesiastes 4:2-3; 6:3-5 | Parallel and allusion: the cursing of the day of birth |
| 21:1-10 | 2 Kings 25:1-7; Jeremiah 37:3-10; 38:2-3; 52:1-11; Exodus 14:13-14; Deuteronomy 30:15-20; Jeremiah 38:17-23 | Parallel and allusion: Zedekiah's inquiry, and the way of life and the way of death |
| 21:11-14 | Jeremiah 22:1-5; 4:4; 7:5-7; Isaiah 1:16-17; Psalm 72:1-4; Jeremiah 22:7 | Parallel and allusion: justice in the morning, and the fire in the forest |
| 22:1-9 | Jeremiah 21:11-12; 7:5-7; Genesis 22:16; Hebrews 6:13; 1 Kings 7:2; Deuteronomy 29:24-28; 1 Kings 9:8-9 | Parallel and allusion: justice and righteousness for the house of the king, and the nations who ask why |
| 22:10-19 | 2 Kings 23:30-34; 23:34 to 24:7; 2 Chronicles 36:1-8; Jeremiah 36:30; 1 Kings 21:1-24; Proverbs 14:31; Habakkuk 2:9-12 | Parallel and allusion: Shallum, Jehoiakim's palace, and "to know me" |
| 22:20-30 | 2 Kings 24:6-17; 2 Chronicles 36:9-10; Haggai 2:23; Jeremiah 13:18; 24:1; 52:31-34; Matthew 1:11-12 | Parallel and allusion: Coniah the signet ring, and the childless man |
| 23:1-8 | Ezekiel 34:1-31; Isaiah 11:1-5; 4:2; Jeremiah 33:14-16; Zechariah 3:8; 6:12; Jeremiah 16:14-15; John 10:11; Luke 1:32-33 | Parallel and allusion: the shepherds, the righteous Branch, and the new exodus |
| 23:9-15 | Jeremiah 5:30-31; 6:13-15; 8:10-12; 14:13-16; Isaiah 28:7; Ezekiel 13:1-16; Lamentations 2:14; Genesis 19:24-25 | Parallel and allusion: the prophets who commit adultery and walk in lies |
| 23:16-32 | Jeremiah 14:13-16; 27:9-18; 29:8-9, 21-23; 30:23-24; Deuteronomy 13:1-5; 18:20-22; Psalm 139:7-12; Isaiah 55:11; Hebrews 4:12 | Parallel and allusion: the council of the LORD, the false dreams, and the word like fire |
| 23:33-40 | Isaiah 13:1; 14:28; 15:1; 17:1; 21:1; 22:1; 23:1; Nahum 1:1; Habakkuk 1:1; Malachi 1:1; Zechariah 9:1; 12:1 | Parallel: the word \*massa\*, "burden" and "oracle" |
| 24:1-10 | 2 Kings 24:10-17; Jeremiah 29:1-23; 1:10; 18:7-10; 31:28; 31:33-34; 32:39; Ezekiel 11:14-21; Jeremiah 42:9-17 | Parallel and allusion: the two baskets of figs, the exiles and those who remain |
| 25:1-14 | Jeremiah 36:1-32; 46:2; 29:10; 2 Chronicles 36:20-21; Daniel 9:2; Zechariah 1:12; Isaiah 13:1 to 14:23; Jeremiah 50:1 to 51:64; Jeremiah 7:34 | Parallel and allusion: the twenty-three years, Nebuchadnezzar my servant, and the seventy years |
| 25:15-29 | Isaiah 51:17-23; Habakkuk 2:15-16; Psalm 75:8; Lamentations 4:21; Revelation 14:10; 1 Peter 4:17; Jeremiah 49:12; 51:7 | Parallel and allusion: the cup of the wine of wrath, the nations that drink, and Sheshach |
| 25:30-38 | Joel 3:16; Amos 1:2; Hosea 11:10; Jeremiah 23:19-20; 30:23-24; Isaiah 63:1-6; Jeremiah 8:2; 16:4; 46:16; 50:16 | Parallel and allusion: the roar of the LORD from on high, and the lament over the shepherds |
| 26:1-6 | Jeremiah 7:1-15; 36:3; 18:7-10; 1 Samuel 4:1-11; Psalm 78:60; Micah 3:12 | Parallel: the temple sermon told again, and Shiloh |
| 26:7-24 | Micah 3:12; Jeremiah 36:1-32; 2 Kings 22:12-14; 25:22; Jeremiah 39:14; 40:5; Matthew 23:34-37; Acts 7:52 | Parallel and allusion: the trial, Micah, and Uriah |
| 27:1-11 | Jeremiah 28:1-17; 25:9-14; 43:10; Daniel 2:37-38; 4:17; Isaiah 45:1-7; Jeremiah 14:13-16; 23:25-32 | Parallel and allusion: the yoke, Nebuchadnezzar my servant, and the false prophets of the nations |
| 27:12-22 | 2 Kings 24:13; 25:13-17; Jeremiah 28:3, 6; 52:17-23; 1 Kings 7:15-50; Ezra 1:7-11; Daniel 1:1-2 | Parallel and allusion: the vessels of the temple, and their return |
| 28:1-17 | Deuteronomy 18:20-22; 13:1-5; Jeremiah 27:2-22; 23:9-40; 29:21-23; 1 Kings 22:11, 24-28; Isaiah 8:20; Micah 3:5 | Parallel and allusion: Hananiah, the broken yoke-bars, and the test of the prophet |
| 29:1-14 | Jeremiah 24:1-10; 25:11-12; 31:15-22; 32:15; 33:6-9; Deuteronomy 4:29; Isaiah 65:21; Ezra 1:1-4; 2 Chronicles 36:21-23 | Parallel and allusion: the letter to the exiles, the welfare of the city, and the seventy years |
| 29:15-23 | Jeremiah 24:8-10; 23:13-14; Leviticus 20:10; Daniel 3:19-30; Deuteronomy 18:20; Jeremiah 14:13-16 | Parallel and allusion: the vile figs, and the two false prophets in Babylon |
